and
s\geq s_{0}+\frac{3}{2}

\big\lVert[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{{\bm{\alpha}}}\partial_{x_{i}},{\bm{u}}_{i}]h\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}H^{s_{0}}_{\bm{x}}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}H^{s}_{\bm{x}}}+\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}H^{s-1}_{\bm{x}}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}H^{s_{0}+1}_{\bm{x}}}\lesssim\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,1}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,1}}.

If
j=k=2
, and since
|{\bm{\alpha}}|\leq s-2
and
s\geq s_{0}+\frac{3}{2}
,

\displaystyle\big\lVert[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{{\bm{\alpha}}}\partial_{x_{i}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j-1},{\bm{u}}_{i}]h\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\big\lVert[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{{\bm{\alpha}}}\partial_{x_{i}},{\bm{u}}_{i}]\partial_{\varrho}h\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert\partial_{\bm{x}}^{{\bm{\alpha}}}\partial_{x_{i}}(h\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}_{i})\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\lVert\partial_{\varrho}h\big\rVert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}H^{s_{0}}_{\bm{x}}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}H^{s-1}_{\bm{x}}}+\big\lVert\partial_{\varrho}h\big\rVert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}H^{s-2}_{\bm{x}}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}H^{s_{0}+1}_{\bm{x}}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}H^{s_{0}}_{\bm{x}}}\big\lVert\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}H^{s-1}_{\bm{x}}}+\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}H^{s-1}_{\bm{x}}}\big\lVert\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}H^{s_{0}}_{\bm{x}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,1}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,2}}.

Finally, we have immediately

\displaystyle\big\lVert[{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j-1},\underline{{\bm{u}}}]\cdot{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{j-2,\infty}_{\varrho}}\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j-2}},

\displaystyle\big\lVert{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j-1}(\underline{h}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{{\bm{\alpha}}}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}})\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{j-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,j}}.

Altogether, we find that for any
1\leq j\leq k

(3.9)

\big\lVert R_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim(\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{k-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k-2,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}})\big(\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-2}}\big).

Estimate of
{\bm{r}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}
and
r_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}
for
0\leq j\leq k
.
We have (
3.7a
) with

\displaystyle{\bm{r}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}

\displaystyle:=-[{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\underline{{\bm{u}}}]{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{{\bm{\alpha}}}h-{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{{\bm{\alpha}}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}(\underline{h}{\bm{u}})-(\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\bm{u}})h,

\displaystyle r_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}

\displaystyle:=-[\partial^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot;{\bm{u}},h]+(\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}h.

We have immediately (since
|{\bm{\alpha}}|+j\leq s
,
j\leq k
, and using Lemma
A.1
)

\displaystyle\big\lVert[{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\underline{{\bm{u}}}]{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}h\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}},

\displaystyle\big\lVert\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}(\underline{h}{\bm{u}})\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{k,\infty}_{\varrho}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}},

\displaystyle\big\lVert(\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\bm{u}})h\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{2},1}}.

By Lemma
A.9
and since
|{\bm{\alpha}}|+j+1\leq s+1
,
j\leq k\leq s
,
s+1\geq s_{0}+\frac{5}{2}
, we find for
2\leq k\leq s

\big\lVert[\partial^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot;{\bm{u}},h]\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}},

and we have by Lemma
A.1

\big\lVert(\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+\frac{3}{2},1}}.

Altogether, we find that for any
0\leq j\leq k

(3.10)

\displaystyle\big\lVert{\bm{r}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{k,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}})\big(\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}\big),

(3.11)

\displaystyle\big\lVert r_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}.

Estimate of
{\bm{R}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},0}
.
The precise expression of the second remainder in (
3.5a
) is the following:

{\bm{R}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},0}:=-\big([{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}},{\bm{u}}]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}+\kappa[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}},\tfrac{1}{\underline{h}+h}](\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}){\bm{u}}+\tfrac{\kappa}{\underline{h}+h}\big([{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}},\nabla_{\bm{x}}h]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}.

By Lemma
A.7
(
2
) and Lemma
A.1

we have

\displaystyle\big\lVert\big([{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}},{\bm{u}}]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}H^{s_{0}+1}_{\bm{x}}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}H^{s}_{\bm{x}}}\lesssim\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+\frac{3}{2},1}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,0}}.

Next, appealing again to
A.7
(
2
), we have

\displaystyle\kappa\big\lVert[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}},\tfrac{1}{\underline{h}+h}](\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}){\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\tfrac{1}{\underline{h}+h}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}H^{s_{0}}_{\bm{x}}}\big\lVert(\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}){\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}H^{s-1}_{\bm{x}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\tfrac{1}{\underline{h}+h}\big\rVert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}H^{s-1}_{\bm{x}}}\big\lVert(\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}){\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}H^{s_{0}}_{\bm{x}}}.

Now, by Lemma
A.2
(
2
) and Lemma
A.4
, one has for any
t\geq 0
that

\displaystyle\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\tfrac{1}{\underline{h}+h}\big\rvert_{H^{t}_{\bm{x}}}

\displaystyle=\big\lvert\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h}{(\underline{h}+h)^{2}}\big\rvert_{H^{t}_{\bm{x}}}\leq\big\lvert\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h}{\underline{h}^{2}}\big\rvert_{H^{t}_{\bm{x}}}+\big\lvert(\tfrac{1}{\underline{h}^{2}}-\tfrac{1}{(\underline{h}+h)^{2}})\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rvert_{H^{t}_{\bm{x}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(h_{\star})^{-2}\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rvert_{H^{t}_{\bm{x}}}+\big\lvert\tfrac{1}{\underline{h}^{2}}-\tfrac{1}{(\underline{h}+h)^{2}}\big\rvert_{H^{s_{0}}_{\bm{x}}}\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rvert_{H^{t}_{\bm{x}}}+\left\langle\big\lvert\tfrac{1}{\underline{h}^{2}}-\tfrac{1}{(\underline{h}+h)^{2}}\big\rvert_{H^{t}_{\bm{x}}}\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rvert_{H^{s_{0}}_{\bm{x}}}\right\rangle_{t>s_{0}}

(3.12)

\displaystyle\leq C(h_{\star},\big\lvert h\big\rvert_{H^{s_{0}}_{\bm{x}}})\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rvert_{H^{t}_{\bm{x}}},

where in the last step we used that, by Lemma
A.4
,

\big\lvert\tfrac{1}{\underline{h}^{2}}-\tfrac{1}{(\underline{h}+h)^{2}}\big\rvert_{H^{s_{0}}_{\bm{x}}}\leq C(h_{\star},\big\lvert h\big\rvert_{H^{s_{0}}_{\bm{x}}})

and, provided that
t>s_{0}
,

\displaystyle\big\lvert\tfrac{1}{\underline{h}^{2}}-\tfrac{1}{(\underline{h}+h)^{2}}\big\rvert_{H^{t}_{\bm{x}}}\leq\big\lvert\tfrac{1}{\underline{h}^{2}}-\tfrac{1}{(\underline{h}+h)^{2}}\big\rvert_{H^{s_{0}}_{\bm{x}}}+\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\tfrac{1}{(\underline{h}+h)^{2}}\big\rvert_{H^{t-1}_{\bm{x}}},

and a finite induction on
t
, until
\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\tfrac{1}{(\underline{h}+h)^{2}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}=\big\lvert\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h}{\underline{h}+h}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}\leq h_{\star}^{-2}\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}
.
Then, by Lemma
A.2
(
2
) and Lemma
A.1
, we have

\displaystyle\big\lVert(\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}){\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}H^{s-1}_{\bm{x}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}H^{s-1}_{\bm{x}}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}H^{s_{0}+1}_{\bm{x}}}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}H^{s_{0}}_{\bm{x}}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}H^{s}_{\bm{x}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,0}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+\frac{3}{2},1}}+\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+\frac{3}{2},1}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,0}}

and

\big\lVert(\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}){\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}H^{s_{0}}_{\bm{x}}}\lesssim\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}H^{s_{0}}_{\bm{x}}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}H^{s_{0}+1}_{\bm{x}}}\lesssim\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+\frac{3}{2},1}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+\frac{3}{2},1}}.

Finally, we have by Lemma
A.7
(
2
) and Lemma
A.1

\displaystyle\big\lVert\big([{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}},\nabla_{\bm{x}}h]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}H^{s_{0}+1}_{\bm{x}}}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}H^{s-1}_{\bm{x}}}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}H^{s}_{\bm{x}}}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}H^{s_{0}}_{\bm{x}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+\frac{3}{2},1}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,0}}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{H^{s,0}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+\frac{3}{2},1}}.

Collecting the estimates above and using that
s\geq s_{0}+\frac{3}{2}
, we obtain

(3.13)

\big\lVert{\bm{R}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},0}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,0}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,1}}+\kappa C(h_{\star},\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{2},1}})\big(\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,1}}^{2}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{H^{s,1}}\big)\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,1}}.

Estimate of
{\bm{R}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}
for
1\leq j\leq k
.
The explicit expression of the second remainder in (
3.6a
) is the following

{\bm{R}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}:=-\big([{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}+\kappa[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\tfrac{1}{\underline{h}+h}]\big((\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}){\bm{u}}\big)+\tfrac{\kappa}{\underline{h}+h}\big([{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\nabla_{\bm{x}}h]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}\\
+{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}\left(\frac{\rho_{0}}{\varrho}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{1}}\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}+\frac{1}{\varrho}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\dd\varrho^{\prime}\right).

By Lemma
A.8
we have for
s\geq s_{0}+\frac{3}{2}
and since
0\leq|{\bm{\alpha}}|\leq s-j
and
j\leq k
with
k\geq 2
, that

\big\lVert\big([{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},{\bm{u}}]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k}}.

Then,

\big\lVert\big([{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\underline{{\bm{u}}}]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}=\big\lVert[{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\underline{{\bm{u}}}]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
\lesssim\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{j-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,j-1}}.

Next, using Lemma
A.3
,

\displaystyle\big\lVert[{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\tfrac{1}{\underline{h}}]{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}\big((\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}){\bm{u}}\big)\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim C(h_{\star})\big\lvert\underline{h}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{j-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}\big\lVert(\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}){\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C(h_{\star})\big\lvert\underline{h}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{j-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,j}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,j}},

and by Lemma
A.8
, since
s\geq s_{0}+\frac{3}{2}
and
2\leq j\leq s
,
|{\bm{\alpha}}|+j\leq s
, Lemma
A.6
and Lemma
A.3
,

\displaystyle\big\lVert[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\tfrac{1}{\underline{h}+h}-\tfrac{1}{\underline{h}}]\big((\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}){\bm{u}}\big)\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\lVert\tfrac{1}{\underline{h}+h}-\tfrac{1}{\underline{h}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big\lVert(\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}){\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,\min(\{k,s-1\})}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C(h_{\star},\big\lvert\underline{h}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k-1,\infty}_{\varrho}},\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}})\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}^{2}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}.

By Lemma
A.8
we have for
s\geq s_{0}+\frac{3}{2}
and since
|{\bm{\alpha}}|+j\leq s
and
2\leq j\leq s

\big\lVert\big([{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\nabla_{\bm{x}}h]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}.

We have immediately since
|{\bm{\alpha}}|\leq s-j\leq s-1
,

\big\lVert{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}\Big(\frac{\rho_{0}}{\varrho}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{1}}\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}\Big)\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\big\lvert{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}\lesssim\big\lvert\eta\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{H^{s}_{\bm{x}}}

and since
(|{\bm{\alpha}}|+1)+(j-1)\leq s
,

\big\lVert{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}\Big(\frac{1}{\varrho}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\dd\varrho^{\prime}\Big)\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}\big\lVert\partial_{\varrho}^{i}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\ \lesssim\big\lVert\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s,j-1}}.

Collecting the estimates above we obtain for
1\leq j\leq k

(3.14)

\big\lVert{\bm{R}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\big\lvert\eta\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{H^{s}_{\bm{x}}}+\big\lVert\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}+\big(\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\\
+\kappa C(h_{\star},\big\lvert\underline{h}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k-1,\infty}_{\varrho}},\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}})\big(\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}^{2}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}.

We infer the bound (
3.5b
) from (
3.8
) and (
3.13
), the bound (
3.6b
) from (
3.9
) and (
3.14
), and the bound (
3.7b
) from (
3.10
) and (
3.11
), and the proof is complete.
∎

3.3.
A priori energy estimates

In this section we provide
a priori
energy estimates associated with the equations featured in Lemma
3.3
.
We start with the transport-diffusion equations in (
3.6a
) and (
3.7a
), which we rewrite as

(3.15)

\partial_{t}\dot{h}+{\bm{u}}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{h}=\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}\dot{h}+r+\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{r}}.

Lemma 3.4
.

There exists a universal constant
C_{0}>0
such that for any
\kappa>0
and
T>0
, for any
{\bm{u}}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\Omega))
with
\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}\in L^{1}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\Omega))
, for any
(r,{\bm{r}})\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
and for any
\dot{h}\in L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
with
\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{h}\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
, such that (
3.15
) holds in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{1,0}(\Omega)^{\prime})
,
we have

(3.16)

\big\lVert\dot{h}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{h}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}\\
\leq C_{0}\big(\big\lVert\dot{h}\big|_{t=0}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert r\big\rVert_{L^{1}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}+\kappa^{-1/2}\big\lVert{\bm{r}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}\big)\\
\times\exp\Big(C_{0}\int_{0}^{T}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\dd t\Big).

Proof.

Testing the equation against
\dot{h}
and integrating by parts (with respect to the variable
{\bm{x}}
) yields

\frac{1}{2}\frac{\dd}{\dd t}\big\lVert\dot{h}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{h}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\iint_{\Omega}(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}})\dot{h}^{2}\dd{\bm{x}}\dd\varrho+\iint_{\Omega}r\dot{h}\dd{\bm{x}}\dd\varrho-\iint_{\Omega}{\bm{r}}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{h}\dd{\bm{x}}\dd\varrho.

The estimate follows from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Gronwall’s Lemma.
∎

Next, we consider system (
3.5a
), which we rewrite as

(3.17)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\dot{\eta}+(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}+\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\dd\varrho^{\prime}+\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\dot{\bm{u}}\dd\varrho^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}+R,

\displaystyle\varrho\left(\partial_{t}\dot{\bm{u}}+\big(({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h}{\underline{h}+h})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big)\dot{\bm{u}}\right)+{\rho_{0}}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}+\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\dd\varrho^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\varrho\nu\Delta_{\bm{x}}\dot{\bm{u}}+{\bm{R}}.

For the sake of readability, we introduce the following notations

(3.18)

X^{0}:=\mathcal{C}^{0}([\rho_{0},\rho_{1}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\times L^{2}(\Omega)^{d};\quad X^{1}:=\mathcal{C}^{0}([\rho_{0},\rho_{1}];H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\times H^{1,0}(\Omega)^{d}.

Lemma 3.5
.

Let
h_{\star},h^{\star},\underline{M}>0
be fixed. There exists
C(h_{\star},h^{\star},M)>0
such that for any
\kappa>0
and
\nu\in[0,1]
, for any
(\underline{h},\underline{{\bm{u}}})\in W^{1,\infty}((\rho_{0},\rho_{1}))
,
for any
T>0
and

(h,{\bm{u}})\in L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{1,\infty}(\Omega))
with
\Delta_{\bm{x}}h\in L^{1}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\Omega))

satisfying (
3.1
) and, for almost every
t\in[0,T]
, the upper bound

\big\lVert h(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\bm{x}}L^{2}_{\varrho}}+\nu^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq M

and the lower and upper bounds

\forall({\bm{x}},\varrho)\in\Omega,\qquad h_{\star}\leq\underline{h}(\varrho)+h(t,{\bm{x}},\varrho)\leq h^{\star};

and for any
(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}})\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];X^{0})\cap L^{2}(0,T;X^{1})
, with
X^{0},X^{1}
in (
3.18
), and
(R,{\bm{R}})\in L^{2}(0,T;X^{0})
satisfying system (
3.17
) in
L^{2}(0,T;X^{1})^{\prime}
, the following estimate holds:

\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta}(t,\cdot),\dot{\bm{u}}(t,\cdot))^{1/2}+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}_{\bm{x}})}+\nu\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\\
\leq\left(\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta}(0,\cdot),\dot{\bm{u}}(0,\cdot))^{1/2}+C\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{E}(R(\tau,\cdot),{\bm{R}}(\tau,\cdot))^{1/2}\dd\tau\right)\\
\times\exp\Big(C\int_{0}^{t}\big(1+\kappa^{-1}\big\lVert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}(\tau,\cdot)\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\bm{x}}L^{2}_{\varrho}}^{2}\big)\dd\tau\Big),

where we denote

\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}}):=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dot{\eta}^{2}+\varrho(\underline{h}+h)\big|\dot{\bm{u}}\big|^{2}\dd{\bm{x}}\dd\varrho\ +\ \frac{\rho_{0}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dot{\eta}^{2}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\dd{\bm{x}}.

Proof.

We test the first equation against
\dot{\eta}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{1,0}(\Omega))
, its trace on
\{({\bm{x}},\rho_{0}),{\bm{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
against
\rho_{0}\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
, and the second equation against
(\underline{h}+h)\dot{\bm{u}}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{1,0}(\Omega))
. This yields, after integration by parts

\displaystyle\frac{\dd}{\dd t}\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}})+\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\rho_{0}\kappa\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}^{2}+\nu\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\Omega}\varrho(\underline{h}+h)|\partial_{x_{i}}\dot{\bm{u}}|^{2}\dd{\bm{x}}\dd\varrho

\displaystyle=-\left((\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta},\dot{\eta}\right)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}-\left(\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\,d\varrho^{\prime},\dot{\eta}\right)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle{\rm(i)}

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\dot{\bm{u}}\,d\varrho^{\prime},\dot{\eta}\right)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big(R,\dot{\eta}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle{\rm(ii)}

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\varrho(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\bm{u}},(\underline{h}+h)\dot{\bm{u}}\right)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\kappa\left(\varrho(\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}})\dot{\bm{u}},\dot{\bm{u}}\right)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle{\rm(iii)}

\displaystyle\quad-\big(\rho_{0}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}},(\underline{h}+h)\dot{\bm{u}}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}-\big(\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\,d\varrho^{\prime},(\underline{h}+h)\dot{\bm{u}}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle{\rm(iv)}

\displaystyle\quad-\nu\big(\varrho(\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\cdot\nabla)\dot{\bm{u}},\dot{\bm{u}}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big(\varrho{\bm{R}},(\underline{h}+h)\dot{\bm{u}}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle{\rm(v)}

\displaystyle\quad-\rho_{0}\left(\big((\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\big)\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}},\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\right)_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}-\rho_{0}\left(\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\dd\varrho^{\prime},\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\right)_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}

\displaystyle{\rm(vi)}

\displaystyle\quad-\rho_{0}\left(\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\dot{\bm{u}}\dd\varrho^{\prime},\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\right)_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}+\rho_{0}\left(R\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}},\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\right)_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}

\displaystyle{\rm(vii)}

\displaystyle\quad+\tfrac{1}{2}(\varrho({\partial}_{t}h)\dot{\bm{u}},\dot{\bm{u}})_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

\displaystyle{\rm(viii)}

We consider first the second terms in (i) and (vi). We have by an immediate application of Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the continuous embedding
L^{\infty}((\rho_{0},\rho_{1}))\subset L^{2}((\rho_{0},\rho_{1}))

\displaystyle\left|\left(\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\,d\varrho^{\prime},\dot{\eta}\right)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\right|+\rho_{0}\left|\left(\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\,d\varrho^{\prime},\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\right)_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}\right|

(3.19)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\big\lVert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\bm{x}}L^{2}_{\varrho}}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\Big(\big\lVert\dot{\eta}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lvert\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}\Big).

Notice that the right-hand side (
3.19
) cannot be bounded by the energy functional
\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}})
, and this is exactly the point where we use the assumption
\kappa>0
. Let us now estimate all other terms.

Using integration by parts in the variable
{\bm{x}}
, we estimate the first addend of (i) and (vi) as follows:

\left|\left((\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta},\dot{\eta}\right)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\right|+\rho_{0}\left|\left(\big((\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\big)\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}},\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\right)_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}\right|\\
\lesssim\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\big\lVert\dot{\eta}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{\infty}_{\bm{x}}}\big\lvert\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}^{2}.

The contributions in (iii) and (viii) compensate after integration by parts in
{\bm{x}}
, using the first equation in (
3.1
).
Now consider the first addend of (ii) together with the second addend of (iv). By application of Fubini’s theorem
we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{1}}\left(\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}(\varrho^{\prime})\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}\,\right)\cdot(\underline{h}+h)(\varrho)\dot{\bm{u}}(\varrho)\,\dd\varrho\,\dd{\bm{x}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{1}}\left(\int_{\varrho^{\prime}}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{h}+h)(\varrho)\dot{\bm{u}}(\varrho)\,\dd\varrho\right)\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}(\varrho^{\prime})\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}\,\dd{\bm{x}}

and hence, integrating by parts in
{\bm{x}}
, we infer

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{1}}\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{h}+h)(\varrho^{\prime})\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\dot{\bm{u}}(\varrho^{\prime})\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}\,\dot{\eta}(\varrho)\,\dd\varrho\,\dd{\bm{x}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{1}}\left(\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}(\varrho^{\prime})\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}\,\right)\cdot(\underline{h}+h)(\varrho)\dot{\bm{u}}(\varrho)\,\dd\varrho\,\dd{\bm{x}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\quad=\Bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{1}}\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}(\nabla_{\bm{x}}h)(\varrho^{\prime})\cdot\dot{\bm{u}}(\varrho^{\prime})\dot{\eta}(\varrho)\,\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}\,\dd\varrho\,\dd{\bm{x}}\Bigg|\lesssim\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\bm{x}}L^{2}_{\varrho}}\big\lVert\dot{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\big\lVert\dot{\eta}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

Concerning first addend of (iv) and the first addend of (vii), we have after integrating by parts with respect to the
{\bm{x}}
variable and using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

\displaystyle\left|-\left(\rho_{0}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}},(\underline{h}+h)\dot{\bm{u}}\right)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}-\rho_{0}\left(\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\dot{\bm{u}}\dd\varrho,\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\right)_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}\right|

\displaystyle\quad=\rho_{0}\left|\left(\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{1}}(\nabla_{\bm{x}}h)\cdot\dot{\bm{u}}\dd\varrho,\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\right)_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}\right|\lesssim\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\bm{x}}L^{2}_{\varrho}}\big\lVert\dot{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\big\lvert\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}.

Concerning the first addend of (v), we have for an arbitrarily large constant
K>0
,

\nu\left|\big(\varrho(\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\cdot\nabla)\dot{\bm{u}},\dot{\bm{u}}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\right|\leq\frac{1}{2K}\nu\big\lVert\nabla\dot{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\frac{K\rho_{1}^{2}}{2}\nu\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}^{2}\big\lVert\dot{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}.

The last contributions, namely

\Big|\big(R,\eta\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big({\bm{R}},\varrho(\underline{h}+h)\dot{\bm{u}}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+{\rho_{0}\big(R\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}},\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big)_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}}\Big|,

are easily controlled by means of Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
Collecting all of the above, and using that

\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}})\approx\big\lVert\dot{\eta}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\big\lVert\dot{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\big\lvert\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}^{2}

and

\nu\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\Omega}\varrho(\underline{h}+h)|\partial_{x_{i}}\dot{\bm{u}}|^{2}\dd{\bm{x}}\dd\varrho\gtrsim\nu\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

since
\rho_{0}h_{\star}\leq\varrho(\underline{h}+h)\leq\rho_{1}h^{\star}
, we obtain (choosing
K
sufficiently large)

\frac{\dd}{\dd t}\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}})+\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\rho_{0}\kappa\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}^{2}\\
\leq C\,\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}})+C\big\lVert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\bm{x}}L^{2}_{\varrho}}\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}})^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
+C\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}})^{1/2}\mathcal{E}(R,{\bm{R}})^{1/2},

with
C=C(h_{\star},h^{\star},M)
. We deduce (augmenting
C
if necessary)

\frac{\dd}{\dd t}\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}})+\frac{\kappa}{2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\rho_{0}\kappa\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}^{2}\\
\leq C\,\big(1+\kappa^{-1}\big\lVert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\bm{x}}L^{2}_{\varrho}}^{2}\big)\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}})+C\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}})^{1/2}\mathcal{E}(R,{\bm{R}})^{1/2},

and the desired estimate follows by Gronwall’s inequality.
∎

3.4.
Large-time existence; proof of Theorem
2.1

We prove the large-time existence and energy estimates on solutions to the regularized system (
3.1
) in the following result. Compared with Proposition
3.1
, we provide an existence time which is uniformly bounded (from below) with respect to the artificial regularization parameter
\nu>0
, and specify the dependency with respect to the diffusivity parameter
\kappa
, in relation with the size of the data. It is in this sense that the existence of strong solutions to the hydrostatic system holds for
large
times. We then complete the proof of Theorem
2.1
at the end of this section.

Proposition 3.6
.

Let
s,k\in\mathbb{N}
be such that
s>2+\frac{d}{2}
,
2\leq k\leq s
, and
\underline{M},M^{\star},h_{\star},h^{\star}>0
. Then, there exists
C>0
such that, for any
0<\nu\leq\kappa\leq 1
, and

•

for any
(\underline{h},\underline{{\bm{u}}})\in W^{k,\infty}((\rho_{0},\rho_{1}))
such that

\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{k,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}\leq\underline{M}\,;

•

for any initial data
(h_{0},{\bm{u}}_{0})=(h_{0}({\bm{x}},\varrho),{\bm{u}}_{0}({\bm{x}},\varrho))\in H^{s,k}(\Omega)
with

M_{0}:=\big\lVert\eta_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lvert\eta_{0}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{H^{s}_{\bm{x}}}+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert h_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\leq M^{\star},

and

\forall({\bm{x}},\varrho)\in\Omega,\qquad h_{\star}\leq\underline{h}(\varrho)+h_{0}({\bm{x}},\varrho)\leq h^{\star},

the following holds. Denoting

T^{-1}=C\,\big(1+\kappa^{-1}\big(\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}}^{2}+M_{0}^{2}\big)\big),

there exists a unique strong solution
(h,{\bm{u}})\in\mathcal{C}([0,T];H^{s,k}(\Omega)^{1+d})
to the Cauchy problem associated with (
3.1
) and initial data
(h,{\bm{u}})\big|_{t=0}=(h_{0},{\bm{u}}_{0})
.
Moreover,
h\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{s+1,k}(\Omega))
and one has, for any
t\in[0,T]
, the lower and the upper bounds

\forall({\bm{x}},\varrho)\in\Omega,\qquad h_{\star}/2\leq\underline{h}(\varrho)+h(t,{\bm{x}},\varrho)\leq 2\,h^{\star},

and the estimate

\mathcal{F}(t):=\big\lVert\eta(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lvert\eta\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}(t,\cdot)\big\rvert_{H^{s}_{\bm{x}}}+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert h(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\\
+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{s,k})}+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{s}_{\bm{x}})}+\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{s,k})}\leq CM_{0}.

Proof.

Let us denote by
T^{\star}\in(0,+\infty]
the maximal time of existence of
(h,{\bm{u}})\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T^{\star});H^{s,k}(\Omega))
as provided by Proposition
3.1
, and

T_{\star}=\sup\Big\{0<T<T^{\star}\ :\ \forall t\in(0,T),\qquad h_{\star}/2\leq\underline{h}(\varrho)+h(t,{\bm{x}},\varrho)\leq 2\,h^{\star}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathcal{F}(t)\leq C_{0}M_{0}\Big\},

where
C_{0}>1
will be determined later on.
By the continuity in time of the solution, and using that the linear operator
h\mapsto\eta:=\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}h(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\dd\varrho^{\prime}
(resp.
h\mapsto\eta\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}
) is well-defined and bounded from
H^{s,k}(\Omega)
to itself (resp.
H^{s}_{\bm{x}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
) we have
T_{\star}>0
.
Using Lemma
3.3
,
3.4
and
3.5
and, therein, the inequalities
\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}=\big\lVert{\partial}_{\varrho}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}\leq\big\lVert\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}
, and (since
\nu\leq\kappa
)
\nu^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}
, we find that there exists
c_{0}>1
depending only on
\rho_{0}h_{\star},\rho_{1}h^{\star}
; and
C>0
depending on
\underline{M},h_{\star},h^{\star},C_{0}M_{0}
such that for any
0<t<T_{\star}
,

(3.20)

\big\lVert\eta(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,0}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,0}}+\big\lvert\eta\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}(t,\cdot)\big\rvert_{H^{s}_{\bm{x}}}+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{s,0})}+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{s}_{\bm{x}})}\\
\leq c_{0}\left(\big\lVert\eta_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s,0}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s,0}}+\big\lvert\eta_{0}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{H^{s}_{\bm{x}}}+C\,C_{0}M_{0}\,\big(t+\sqrt{t}\big)\right)\\
\times\exp\Big(C\int_{0}^{t}\big(1+\kappa^{-1}\big\lVert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\bm{x}}L^{2}_{\varrho}}^{2}\big)\dd\tau\Big);

and (using a slightly adapted version of Lemma
3.5
which does not involve the trace of
\partial_{\varrho}^{j}\eta
at the surface) for any
1\leq j\leq k

(3.21)

\big\lVert\partial_{\varrho}^{j}\eta(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s-j,0}}+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\partial_{\varrho}^{j}\eta\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{s-j,0})}\\
\leq\left(\big\lVert\partial_{\varrho}^{j}\eta(0,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s-j,0}}+C\,C_{0}M_{0}\,\big(t+\sqrt{t}\big)\right)\\
\times\exp\Big(C\int_{0}^{t}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}(\tau,\cdot)\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\dd\tau\Big),

and

(3.22)

\big\lVert\partial_{\varrho}^{j}{\bm{u}}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s-j,0}}+\nu^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\partial_{\varrho}^{j}{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{s-j,0})}\\
\leq\left(\big\lVert\partial_{\varrho}^{j}{\bm{u}}(0,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s-j,0}}+C\,C_{0}M_{0}\,\big(t+\sqrt{t}\big)\right)\\
\times\exp\Big(C\int_{0}^{t}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\big({\bm{u}}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h}{\underline{h}+h}\big)(\tau,\cdot)\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\dd\tau\Big);

and finally for any
0\leq j\leq k

(3.23)

\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert\partial_{\varrho}^{j}h(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s-j,0}}+\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\partial_{\varrho}^{j}h\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{s-j,0})}\\
\leq\left(\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert\partial_{\varrho}^{j}h(0,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s-j,0}}+C\,C_{0}M_{0}\,\big(t+\sqrt{t}\big)\right)\\
\times\exp\Big(C\int_{0}^{t}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}(\tau,\cdot)\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\dd\tau\Big).

By the continuous embeddings
H^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{2},1}\subset L^{\infty}_{\varrho}H^{s_{0}}\subset L^{\infty}(\Omega)
for any
s_{0}>d/2
(see Lemma
A.1
) and since
k\geq 1
and
s>\frac{3}{2}+\frac{d}{2}
, we have

\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\big({\bm{u}}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h}{\underline{h}+h}\big)\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq C(h_{\star})\big(\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\kappa\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}^{2}+\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big).

We deduce that

\mathcal{F}(t)\leq c\Big(M_{0}+C\,C_{0}M_{0}\,\big(t+\sqrt{t}\big)\Big)\times\exp\Big(C\big(t+\sqrt{t}+\kappa^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}\big\lVert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}(\tau,\cdot)\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\bm{x}}L^{2}_{\varrho}}^{2}\dd\tau\big)\Big),

where we recall that
c_{0}>1
depends only on
h_{\star}
and
h^{\star}
; and
C>0
depends on
\underline{M},C_{0}M_{0},h_{\star},h^{\star}
.
Hence choosing
C_{0}=2c_{0}

and using that (by Lemma
A.1
and since
k\geq 2
and
s>\frac{3}{2}+\frac{d}{2}
)

\big\lVert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\bm{x}}L^{2}_{\varrho}}\leq\big\lVert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}L^{\infty}_{\bm{x}}}^{2}\lesssim\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}}^{2}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}^{2}\leq\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}}^{2}+(C_{0}M_{0})^{2},

we find that there exists
C_{0}\geq 1
depending only on
\underline{M},M^{\star},h_{\star},h^{\star}
such that

t\big(1+\kappa^{-1}\big(\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}}^{2}+M_{0}^{2}\big)\big)\leq C_{0}^{-1}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\mathcal{F}(t)\leq\frac{3}{4}C_{0}M_{0}.

Now we remark that since

\partial_{t}h+\underline{{\bm{u}}}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}h=\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}h+g\quad\text{ with }\quad g=-\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot(\underline{h}{\bm{u}}+h{\bm{u}})

and by the positivity of the heat kernel we have

\inf_{\Omega}h(t,\cdot)\geq\inf_{\Omega}h_{0}-\big\lVert g\big\rVert_{L^{1}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))},\qquad\sup_{\Omega}h(t,\cdot)\leq\sup_{\Omega}h_{0}+\big\lVert g\big\rVert_{L^{1}(0,t;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}.

Now, by the continuous embedding
H^{s-1,1}(\Omega)\subset L^{\infty}(\Omega)
(since
s>\frac{3}{2}+\frac{d}{2}
), we have that

\big\lVert g\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\lesssim\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{1,\infty}_{\varrho}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,1}}+\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,1}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,1}}\\
\leq C(\underline{M})(1+\kappa^{-1}M_{0}^{2}).

Hence augmenting
C_{0}
if necessary we find that

t(1+\kappa^{-1}M_{0}^{2})\leq C_{0}^{-1}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\forall({\bm{x}},\varrho)\in\Omega,\quad\frac{2}{3}h_{\star}\leq\underline{h}(\varrho)+h(t,{\bm{x}},\varrho)\leq\frac{3}{2}h^{\star}.

By a continuity argument we infer
T_{\star}\geq\Big(C\big(1+\kappa^{-1}\big(\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}}^{2}+M_{0}^{2}\big)\big)\Big)^{-1}
, and the proof is complete.
∎

Completion of the proof of Theorem
2.1

In order to complete the proof of Theorem
2.1
, there remains to consider vanishing viscosity limit,
\nu\searrow 0
, in Proposition
3.6
. Let us briefly sketch the standard argument.
By Proposition
3.6
, we construct a family
(h_{\nu},{\bm{u}}_{\nu})\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];H^{s,k}(\Omega))
of solutions to (
3.1
) with
(h_{\nu},{\bm{u}}_{\nu})\big|_{t=0}=(h_{0},{\bm{u}}_{0})
indexed by the parameter
\nu>0
. Notice that the time of existence and associated bounds provided by Proposition
3.6
are uniform with respect to the parameter
\nu>0
. Hence by the Banach-Alaoglu theorem there exists a subsequence which converges weakly towards
(h,{\bm{u}})\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s,k}(\Omega)^{1+d})
, satisfying the estimates of Proposition
3.6
. Using the equations, we find that
(\partial_{t}\zeta_{\nu},\partial_{t}{\bm{u}}_{\nu})
are uniformly bounded in
L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s-2,k})
.
The Aubin-Lions lemma (see
[
Simon87
]
) implies that, up to extracting a subsequence, the convergence holds strongly in
(h,{\bm{u}})\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];H^{s^{\prime},k}(B)^{1+d})
for any
0\leq s^{\prime}<s
for any bounded
B\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(\rho_{0},\rho_{1})
. Choosing
s^{\prime}>3/2+d/2
and using Lemma
A.1
and Sobolev embedding, we can pass to the limit in the nonlinear terms of the equation and infer that that
(h,{\bm{u}})
is a strong solution to (
3.1
) with
\nu=0
. Moreover, since
(h,{\bm{u}})\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];H^{s-2,k}(\Omega)^{1+d})
, we have
(h,{\bm{u}})\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];H^{s^{\prime},k}(\Omega)^{1+d})
for any
0\leq s^{\prime}<s
.

Uniqueness of the solution
(h,{\bm{u}})\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s,k}(\Omega)^{1+d})
follows by using Lemma
3.5
on the difference between two solutions, and Gronwall’s Lemma.

There remains to prove that
(h,{\bm{u}})\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];H^{s,k}(\Omega)^{1+d})
.
We prove the equivalent statement that for any
{\bm{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
and
j\in\mathbb{N}
such that
0\leq|{\bm{\alpha}}|+j\leq s
and
0\leq j\leq k
,
({\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}h,{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\bm{u}})\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega)^{1+d})
. By Lemma
3.3
, as long as
\kappa>0
, we can write

\displaystyle\partial_{t}({\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}h)-\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}({\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}h)

\displaystyle=r_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}+\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}\cdot{\bm{r}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j},

\displaystyle\partial_{t}({\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\bm{u}})+({\bm{v}}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}})({\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\bm{u}})

\displaystyle=R_{{\bm{\alpha}},j},

with
(r_{{\bm{\alpha}},j},{\bm{r}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j},R_{{\bm{\alpha}},j})\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))^{1+2d}
and
{\bm{v}}(\cdot,\varrho):=\underline{{\bm{u}}}(\varrho)+\big({\bm{u}}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h}{\underline{h}+h}\big)(\cdot,\varrho)\in W^{k,\infty}((\rho_{0},\rho_{1}))^{d}+L^{2}(0,T;H^{s,k}(\Omega))^{d}
.
In other words,
{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}h
satisfies a heat equation and continuity in time stems from the Duhamel formula, as already used in Proposition
3.1
; and
{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\bm{u}}
satisfies a transport equation and continuity in time is standard, see
e.g.

[
BCD11
]
*Th. 3.19. Let us acknowledge however that our situation is slightly different, since
\Omega
is neither the Euclidean space or the torus, and advection occurs only in the direction
{\bm{x}}
(and not
\varrho
). It is however easy to adapt the proof of
[
BCD11
]
*Th. 3.19 to infer
{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\bm{u}}\in L^{2}(\rho_{0},\rho_{1};\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})))^{d}\subset\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];L^{2}(\Omega))^{d}
from the facts that
R_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\in L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))^{d}\subset L^{2}(\rho_{0},\rho_{1};L^{1}(0,T;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})))^{d}
and
\nabla_{\bm{x}}{\bm{v}}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{s-1,k}(\Omega))^{d\times d}\subset L^{1}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\rho_{0},\rho_{1};H^{s-3/2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})))^{d\times d}
and
s-3/2>d/2
, the continuous embeddings following from Minkowski inequality and Lemma
A.1
.

This concludes the proof of Theorem
2.1
.

4.
The non-hydrostatic system

In this section we study the local well-posedness theory for the non-hydrostatic system in isopycnal coordinates, which we recall below.

(4.1)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}h+\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}\cdot\big((\underline{h}+h)(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}})\big)

\displaystyle=\kappa\Delta_{{\bm{x}}}h,

\displaystyle\varrho\Big(\partial_{t}{\bm{u}}+\big((\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}h}{\underline{h}+h})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}\Big)+\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}P+\frac{\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}\eta}{\underline{h}+h}({\partial}_{\varrho}P+\varrho\underline{h})

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\mu\varrho\Big(\partial_{t}w+\big(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}h}{\underline{h}+h}\big)\cdot\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}w\Big)-\frac{{\partial}_{\varrho}P}{\underline{h}+h}+\frac{\varrho h}{\underline{h}+h}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle-(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}\cdot{\bm{u}}-\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}\eta\cdot({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}^{\prime}+\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}})+\partial_{\varrho}w

\displaystyle=0,\quad\text{(div.-free cond.)}

\displaystyle\eta(\cdot,\varrho)=\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}h(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\dd\varrho^{\prime},\qquad P\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}=0,\qquad w\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{1}}

\displaystyle=0.\quad\text{(bound. cond.)}

4.1.
The pressure reconstruction

The first step of our analysis consists in showing how the pressure variable,
P
, can be uniquely reconstructed (thanks to the “divergence-free” incompressibility constraint) from prognostic variables
{\bm{u}}
,
w
and
h
(or, equivalently,
\eta
), through an elliptic boundary-value problem.
Differentiating the “divergence-free” incompressibility constraint in (
4.1
) with respect to time yields

-(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\partial_{t}{\bm{u}}-(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta)\cdot(\partial_{\varrho}\partial_{t}{\bm{u}})+\partial_{\varrho}\partial_{t}w=(\partial_{t}h)(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}})+(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\partial_{t}\eta)\cdot({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}^{\prime}+\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}).

We plug the expressions for
{\partial}_{t}{\bm{u}},{\partial}_{t}w,{\partial}_{t}h,{\partial}_{t}\eta
provided by (
4.1
) inside the above identity. Reorganizing terms, this yields the following

\textstyle(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}P+\frac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta(\partial_{\varrho}P+\varrho\underline{h})}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}\right)+(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta)\cdot\left(\partial_{\varrho}\left(\frac{1}{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}P+\frac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta(\partial_{\varrho}P+\varrho\underline{h})}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}\right)\right)+\partial_{\varrho}\left(\frac{{\partial}_{\varrho}P+\varrho\underline{h}}{\mu\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}\right)\\
\textstyle=-(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\left(\big(({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h}{\underline{h}+h})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}\right)-(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta)\cdot\left(\partial_{\varrho}\left(\big(({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h}{\underline{h}+h})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}\right)\right)\\
+\partial_{\varrho}\left(\big({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h}{\underline{h}+h}\big)\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}w\right)+\Big(\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}h-\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\big((\underline{h}+h)({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}})\big)\Big)(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}})\\
\textstyle+\left(\kappa\nabla_{\bm{x}}\Delta_{\bm{x}}\eta-\nabla_{\bm{x}}\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot((\underline{h}+h)({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}))\dd\varrho^{\prime}\right)\cdot({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}^{\prime}+\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}).

Using that
{\partial}_{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta=-\nabla_{\bm{x}}h
we can rewrite the left-hand side in a compact formulation as

{\rm(LHS)}=\frac{1}{\mu}\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{\mu}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\\
{\partial}_{\varrho}\end{pmatrix}\cdot\left(\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\underline{h}+h}{\varrho}\Id&\frac{\sqrt{\mu}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta}{\varrho}\\
\frac{\sqrt{\mu}\nabla_{\bm{x}}^{\top}\eta}{\varrho}&\frac{1+\mu|\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta|^{2}}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{\mu}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\\
{\partial}_{\varrho}\end{pmatrix}(P+\underline{P}_{\rm eq})\right),

with
\underline{P}_{\rm eq}:=\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\varrho^{\prime}\underline{h}(\varrho^{\prime})\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}
.
As for the right-hand side, we denote

(4.2)

{\bm{u}}_{\star}:=-\kappa\frac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h}{\underline{h}+h},

and we infer

{\rm(RHS)}=-(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\left(\big(({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}+{\bm{u}}_{\star})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}\right)-(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta)\cdot\left(\partial_{\varrho}\left(\big({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}+{\bm{u}}_{\star}\big)\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}{\bm{u}}\right)\right)\\
+\partial_{\varrho}\left(\big({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}+{\bm{u}}_{\star}\big)\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}w\right)-\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot((\underline{h}+h)({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}+{\bm{u}}_{\star}))(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}})\\
\textstyle\quad-\left(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot((\underline{h}+h)({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}+{\bm{u}}_{\star}))\dd\varrho^{\prime}\right)\cdot({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}^{\prime}+\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}).

Notice the identity (reminiscent of (
2.3
))

(4.3)

\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot((\underline{h}+h)({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}+{\bm{u}}_{\star}))\dd\varrho^{\prime}=({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}+{\bm{u}}_{\star})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta-w-w_{\star},

where

(4.4)

w_{\star}:=\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}\eta-\kappa\frac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta}{\underline{h}+h},

which is obtained by integrating with respect to
\varrho
the divergence-free identities

\displaystyle-(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}-(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta)\cdot({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}^{\prime}+\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}})+\partial_{\varrho}w

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle-(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}_{\star}-(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta)\cdot(\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}_{\star})+\partial_{\varrho}w_{\star}

\displaystyle=0,

integrating by parts with respect to
\varrho
, using the boundary condition
w|_{\varrho=\rho_{1}}=0=w_{\star}|_{\varrho=\rho_{1}}
and
h=-{\partial}_{\varrho}\eta
.
Hence the above can be equivalently written as

(4.5)

{\rm(RHS)}=-(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\left(\big({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}+{\bm{u}}_{\star}\big)\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}{\bm{u}}\right)-(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta)\cdot\left(\partial_{\varrho}\left(\big({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}+{\bm{u}}_{\star}\big)\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}{\bm{u}}\right)\right)\\
+\partial_{\varrho}\left(\big({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}+{\bm{u}}_{\star}\big)\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}w\right)-\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot((\underline{h}+h)({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}+{\bm{u}}_{\star}))(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}})\\
-\left(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\left(({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}+{\bm{u}}_{\star})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta-w-w_{\star}\right)\right)\cdot({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}^{\prime}+\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}).

Taking into account the boundary conditions in (
4.1
), we find that the pressure satisfies the following problem (recalling
\underline{P}_{\rm eq}:=\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\varrho^{\prime}\underline{h}(\varrho^{\prime})\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}
):

(4.6)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu}\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{\mu}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\\
{\partial}_{\varrho}\end{pmatrix}\cdot\left(\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\underline{h}+h}{\varrho}\Id&\frac{\sqrt{\mu}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta}{\varrho}\\
\frac{\sqrt{\mu}\nabla_{\bm{x}}^{\top}\eta}{\varrho}&\frac{1+\mu|\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta|^{2}}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{\mu}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\\
{\partial}_{\varrho}\end{pmatrix}(P+\underline{P}_{\rm eq})\right)={\rm(RHS)},\\
P\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}=0,\qquad({\partial}_{\varrho}P)\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{1}}=\rho_{1}h\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{1}}.\end{array}\right.

This boundary value problem corresponds to
[
Desjardins-Lannes-Saut
]
*(7) written in isopycnal coordinates, adapting the boundary conditions to the free-surface framework, and taking into account the effective transport velocities from eddy correlation. Following
[
Desjardins-Lannes-Saut
]
, we shall infer the existence and uniqueness as well as estimates on the pressure
P
from the elliptic theory applied to the above boundary value problem, as stated below.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
s_{0}>d/2
,
s,k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
s\geq s_{0}+\frac{5}{2}
and
1\leq k\leq s
. Let
\underline{M},M,h_{\star}>0
. There exists
C>0
such that for any
\mu\in(0,1]
, and for any
\underline{h},h,\eta
satisfying
the following bound

\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{1\vee k-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}\leq\underline{M},\quad\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,1\vee k-1}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,1\vee k-1}}\leq M;

(where we recall the notation
a\vee b=\max(a,b)
) and the stable stratification assumption

\inf_{({\bm{x}},\varrho)\in\Omega}\underline{h}(\varrho)+h({\bm{x}},\varrho)\geq{h_{\star}};

and for any
(Q_{0},Q_{1},{\bm{R}})\in H^{s,k-1}(\Omega)^{2}\times H^{s,k}(\Omega)^{d+1}
, there exists a unique
P\in H^{s+1,k+1}(\Omega)
solution to

(4.7)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}\cdot\big(A^{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big)=Q_{0}+\sqrt{\mu}\Lambda Q_{1}+\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}\cdot{\bm{R}}\\
P\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}=0,\qquad{\bm{e}}_{d+1}\cdot(A\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P)\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{1}}={\bm{e}}_{d+1}\cdot{\bm{R}}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{1}}\end{array}\right.

where we denote
\Lambda:=(\Id-\Delta_{\bm{x}})^{1/2}
,

\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}:=\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{\mu}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\\
{\partial}_{\varrho}\end{pmatrix}\qquad;\qquad A^{\mu}:=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\underline{h}+h}{\varrho}\Id&\frac{\sqrt{\mu}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta}{\varrho}\\
\frac{\sqrt{\mu}\nabla_{\bm{x}}^{\top}\eta}{\varrho}&\frac{1+\mu|\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta|^{2}}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}\end{pmatrix},

and one has, denoting
\big\lVert(Q_{0},Q_{1},{\bm{R}})\big\rVert_{r,j}:=\big\lVert Q_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{r,j-1}}+\big\lVert Q_{1}\big\rVert_{H^{r,j-1}}+\big\lVert{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}
,

(4.8)

\big\lVert P\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\leq C\,\times\Big(\big\lVert(Q_{0},Q_{1},{\bm{R}})\big\rVert_{s,k}\\
+\big(\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)\,\big\lVert(Q_{0},Q_{1},{\bm{R}})\big\rVert_{s-1,1\vee k-1}\Big)

and, when
k\geq 2
,

(4.9)

\big\lVert P\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}\leq C\big\lVert(Q_{0},Q_{1},{\bm{R}})\big\rVert_{s-1,k-1}.

Proof.

Testing (
4.7
) with
P
, using integration by parts and the boundary conditions, we find

-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{1}}A^{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\cdot\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\,\dd\varrho\,\dd{\bm{x}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{1}}Q_{0}{P}+Q_{1}(\sqrt{\mu}\Lambda{P})-\tilde{\bm{R}}\cdot\nabla_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}^{\mu}P\,\dd\varrho\,\dd{\bm{x}}.

For
(Q_{0},Q_{1},{\bm{R}})\in L^{2}(\Omega)^{2+d+1}
, the existence and uniqueness of a (variational) solution to (
4.7
) in the functional space

H^{1}_{0}(\Omega):=\{P\in L^{2}(\Omega)\ :\ \nabla_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\in L^{2}(\Omega),\ P\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}=0\}

classically follows from the Lax-Milgram Lemma thanks to the boundedness and the coercivity of the matrix
A

(recall that
\underline{h}+h\geq h_{\star}>0
and the embedding of Lemma
A.1
), and the Poincaré inequality

(4.10)

\forall P\in H^{1}_{0}(\Omega),\qquad\big\lVert P\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{1}}\left|\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\partial_{\varrho^{\prime}}P\dd\varrho^{\prime}\right|^{2}\dd\varrho\dd{\bm{x}}\leq(\rho_{1}-\rho_{0})^{2}\big\lVert{\partial}_{\varrho}P\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2},

and we have

(4.11)

\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\big\lVert Q_{0}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert Q_{1}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+{\big\lVert{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}}.

The desired regularity for
(Q_{0},Q_{1},{\bm{R}})\in H^{s,k-1}(\Omega)^{2}\times H^{s,k}(\Omega)^{d+1}
is then deduced following the standard approach for elliptic equations (notice the domain is flat) from the estimates which we obtain below. For more details, we refer for instance to
[
Lannesbook
]
*Chapter 2 where a very similar elliptic problem is thoroughly studied. We now focus on the estimates, assuming
a priori
the needed regularity to justify the following computations.

First, we provide an estimate for
\big\lVert\nabla_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{r,0}(\Omega)}
for
1\leq r\leq s
.
One readily checks that
P_{r}:=\Lambda^{r}P
with
\Lambda^{r}:=(\Id-\Delta_{\bm{x}})^{r/2}
satisfies (
4.7
)
with
Q_{0}\leftarrow\Lambda^{r}Q_{0}
,
Q_{1}\leftarrow\Lambda^{r}Q_{1}
and
{\bm{R}}\leftarrow\Lambda^{r}{\bm{R}}-[\Lambda^{r},A^{\mu}]\nabla_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P
. We focus now on the contribution of
{\bm{P}}_{r}:=[\Lambda^{r},A^{\mu}]\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P
.
By continuous embedding (Lemma
A.1
) and commutator estimates (Lemma
A.7
), we have

\big\lVert{\bm{P}}_{r}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}A^{\mu}\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{2},1}}\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{r-1,0}}+\left\langle\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}A^{\mu}\big\rVert_{H^{r-1,0}}\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{2},1}}\right\rangle_{r>s_{0}+1}.

Hence, using product (Lemma
A.2
,
A.3
) and composition (Lemma
A.4
,
A.6
) estimates, we deduce

(4.12)

\big\lVert{\bm{P}}_{r}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C\,\Big(\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{r-1,0}}+\left\langle\big(\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{r,0}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{r,0}}\big)\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{2},1}}\right\rangle_{r>s_{0}+1}\Big)

with

C=C(h_{\star},\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{1,\infty}_{\varrho}},\big\lVert(h,\sqrt{\mu}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta)\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+\frac{3}{2},1}})
.

Plugging (
4.12
) in (
4.11
) and using continuous embedding (Lemma
A.1
) and
s_{0}+\frac{3}{2}\leq s-1
yields

(4.13)

\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{r,0}}\lesssim\big\lVert Q_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{r,0}}+\big\lVert Q_{1}\big\rVert_{H^{r,0}}+\big\lVert{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{r,0}}+\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{r-1,0}}\ \\
+\ C\,\big(\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{r,0}}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{r,0}}\big)\left\langle\big\lVert\nabla_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{2},1}}\right\rangle_{r>s_{0}+1},

where we denote, here and thereafter,
a\lesssim b
for
a\leq Cb
with

C=C(h_{\star},\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{1,\infty}_{\varrho}},\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,1}},\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,1}})=C(h_{\star},\underline{M},M).

Next we provide an estimate for
\big\lVert\nabla_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{r,1}(\Omega)}
appearing in the above right-hand side. This term involves
{\partial}_{\varrho}^{2}P
, which we control by rewriting (
4.7
) as

(4.14)

\tfrac{1+\mu|\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta|^{2}}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}\partial_{\varrho}^{2}P=-\partial_{\varrho}\big(\tfrac{1+\mu|\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta|^{2}}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}\big)(\partial_{\varrho}P)-\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}\cdot A_{0}^{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P+Q_{0}+\sqrt{\mu}\Lambda Q_{1}+\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}\cdot{\bm{R}}=:\widetilde{\bm{R}}

where we denote

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}\cdot A_{0}^{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P

\displaystyle:=\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}\cdot\left(\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\underline{h}+h}{\varrho}\Id&\frac{\sqrt{\mu}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta}{\varrho}\\
\frac{\sqrt{\mu}\nabla_{\bm{x}}^{\top}\eta}{\varrho}&0\end{pmatrix}\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu}{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\big(h\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}P+(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta)(\partial_{\varrho}P)\big)+\partial_{\varrho}\big(\frac{\mu}{\varrho}(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta)\cdot(\nabla_{\bm{x}}P)\big).

When estimating the above, we use product estimates (Lemma
A.2
) and then continuous embedding (Lemma
A.1
), treating differently terms involving
\Delta_{\bm{x}}P
or
\nabla_{\bm{x}}{\partial}_{\varrho}P
: for instance

\displaystyle\big\lVert\Lambda^{r-1}(h\Delta_{\bm{x}}P)\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{2},1}}\big\lVert\Delta_{\bm{x}}P\big\rVert_{H^{r-1,0}}+\big\langle\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{r-\frac{1}{2},1}}\big\lVert\Delta_{\bm{x}}P\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0},0}}\big\rangle_{r-1>s_{0}};

and terms involving only
\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}P
or
{\partial}_{\varrho}P
: for instance

\displaystyle\big\lVert\Lambda^{r-1}((\Delta_{\bm{x}}\eta)({\partial}_{\varrho}P))\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\lVert\Delta_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{2},1}}\big\lVert{\partial}_{\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{r-1,0}}+\big\langle\big\lVert\Delta_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{r-1,0}}\big\lVert{\partial}_{\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+\frac{1}{2},1}}\big\rangle_{r-1>s_{0}}.

We infer, using Lemma
A.1
,
\mu\in(0,1]
and
s_{0}+\frac{3}{2}\leq s-1
, that for any
1\leq r\leq s
,

(4.15)

\big\lVert\partial_{\varrho}^{2}P\big\rVert_{H^{r-1,0}}\lesssim\big\lVert Q_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{r-1,0}}+\big\lVert Q_{1}\big\rVert_{H^{r,0}}+\big\lVert{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{r,1}}+\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{r,0}}\\
+\left(\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{r,1}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{r,1}}\right)\left\langle\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+1,1}}\right\rangle_{r>s_{0}+1}.

By combining (
4.13
) and (
4.15
) we obtain

\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{r,1}}\lesssim\big\lVert Q_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{r,0}}+\big\lVert Q_{1}\big\rVert_{H^{r,0}}+\big\lVert{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{r,1}}+\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{r-1,0}}\ \\
+\ C\,{\left\langle\left(\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{r,1}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{r,1}}\right)\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{s_{0}+1,1}}\right\rangle_{r>s_{0}+1}}

which, after finite induction on
1\leq r\leq s
and using (
4.11
) for the initialization, yields

(4.16)

\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{r,1}}\lesssim\big\lVert Q_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{r,0}}+\big\lVert Q_{1}\big\rVert_{H^{r,0}}+\big\lVert{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{r,1}}\\
+\left(\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{r,1}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{r,1}}\right)\,\times\left\langle\big\lVert Q_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{r-1,0}}+\big\lVert Q_{1}\big\rVert_{H^{r-1,0}}+\big\lVert{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{r-1,1}}\right\rangle_{r>s_{0}+1}.

This, together with (
4.10
), proves (
4.8
) when
k=1
.

We now proceed to estimate higher
\varrho
-derivatives. In what follows, we denote

C=C(h_{\star},\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{k-1,\infty}_{\varrho}},\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}},\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}})=C(h_{\star},\underline{M},M).

Let
2\leq j\leq k
. By definition, and using
\mu\in(0,1]
, we have

\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{s,j}}\leq\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{s,j-1}}+\big\lVert{\partial}_{\varrho}\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j-1}}\lesssim\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{s,j-1}}+\big\lVert{\partial}_{\varrho}^{2}P\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j-1}}.

We shall also use, when
j\leq k-1
, the corresponding estimate

\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j}}\lesssim\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j-1}}+\big\lVert{\partial}_{\varrho}^{2}P\big\rVert_{H^{s-2,j-1}}.

By using (
4.14
) (according to which
{\partial}_{\varrho}^{2}P=\tfrac{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}{1+\mu|\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta|^{2}}\tilde{\bm{R}}
), and since
1\leq j-1\leq s-1
, using Lemma
A.3
and Lemma
A.5
yields

\big\lVert\partial_{\varrho}^{2}P\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j-1}}\lesssim\big\lVert\tfrac{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}{1+\mu|\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta|^{2}}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j-1}}\big\lVert\widetilde{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j-1}}\leq C\big\lVert\widetilde{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j-1}}.

If moreover
j\leq k-1\leq s-1
, then

\big\lVert\partial_{\varrho}^{2}P\big\rVert_{H^{s-2,j-1}}\lesssim\big\lVert\tfrac{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}{1+\mu|\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta|^{2}}\big\rVert_{H^{s-2,j-1}}\big\lVert\widetilde{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{s-2,j-1}}\leq C\big\lVert\widetilde{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{s-2,j-1}}.

Applying Lemma
A.3
and Lemma
A.5
to
\widetilde{\bm{R}}
defined in (
4.14
), we obtain

\big\lVert\widetilde{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j-1}}\leq\big\lVert Q_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j-1}}+\big\lVert Q_{1}\big\rVert_{H^{s,j-1}}+\big\lVert{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,j}}\\
+C\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{s,j-1}}+C\,\times\left(\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,j}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s,j}}\right)\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j-1}}

and, if moreover
j\leq k-1\leq s-1
,

\big\lVert\widetilde{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{s-2,j-1}}\leq\big\lVert Q_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s-2,j-1}}+\big\lVert Q_{1}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j-1}}+\big\lVert{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j}}+C\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j-1}}.

From the second set of inequalities, (
4.16
) with
r=s-1
and finite induction on
2\leq j\leq k-1
we infer

\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j}}\leq C\,\big(\big\lVert Q_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j-1}}+\big\lVert Q_{1}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j-1}}+\big\lVert{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j}}\big).

Then, from the first set of inequalities, (
4.16
) with
r=s
and the previous result, we infer by finite induction on
2\leq j\leq k

\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{s,j}}\leq C\,\big(\big\lVert Q_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s,j-1}}+\big\lVert Q_{1}\big\rVert_{H^{s,j-1}}+\big\lVert{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,j}}\big)\\
+C\,\big(\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,j}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s,j}}\big)\,\big(\big\lVert Q_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j-2}}+\big\lVert Q_{1}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j-2}}+\big\lVert{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,j-1}}\big).

The result is proved.
∎

We now apply Lemma
4.1
to obtain several estimates on the solution to (
4.5
)-(
4.6
).

Corollary 4.2
.

Let
s_{0}>d/2
,
s,k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
s\geq s_{0}+\frac{5}{2}
and
2\leq k\leq s
. Let
\underline{M},M,h_{\star}>0
. There exists
C>0
such that for any
\mu\in(0,1]
and
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
, for any
(\underline{h},\underline{{\bm{u}}})\in W^{k,\infty}((\rho_{0},\rho_{1}))^{1+d}
, and for any
(h,{\bm{u}},w)\in H^{s+1,k}(\Omega)\times H^{s,k}(\Omega)^{d}\times H^{s,k-1}(\Omega)
satisfying (denoting
\eta(\cdot,\varrho):=\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}h(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\dd\varrho^{\prime}
)

•

the following bounds

\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{k,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}\leq\underline{M},

\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert w\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}\leq M;

•

the stable stratification assumption

\inf_{({\bm{x}},\varrho)\in\Omega}\underline{h}(\varrho)+h({\bm{x}},\varrho)\geq{h_{\star}};

•

the incompressibility condition

-(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}-(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta)\cdot({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}^{\prime}+\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}})+\partial_{\varrho}w=0,

there exists a unique solution
P\in H^{s+1,k+1}(\Omega)
to (
4.5
)-(
4.6
) and one has

(4.17)

\big\lVert P\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\leq C\,(1+\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}})\\
\times\left(\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert({\bm{u}},{\bm{u}}_{\star})\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert(w,w_{\star})\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}\right)

where we recall the notations
{\bm{u}}_{\star}:=-\kappa\frac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h}{\underline{h}+h}
and
w_{\star}:=\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}\eta-\kappa\frac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta}{\underline{h}+h}
.

Moreover, decomposing

P=P_{\rm h}+P_{\rm nh},\qquad P_{\rm h}:=\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\varrho^{\prime}h(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\,\dd\varrho^{\prime},

we have

(4.18)

\big\lVert P_{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P_{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}\leq C\sqrt{\mu}\Big(\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}+\big\lvert\eta\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{H^{s}_{\bm{x}}}\\
+\big\lVert({\bm{u}},{\bm{u}}_{\star})\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert(w,w_{\star})\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}\Big),

and, setting
\Lambda^{\mu}=1+\sqrt{\mu}|D|
,

(4.19)

\big\lVert P_{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P_{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}\leq C\,\mu\,\Big(\big\lVert(\Lambda^{\mu})^{-1}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}+\big\lvert(\Lambda^{\mu})^{-1}\eta\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{H^{s+1}_{\bm{x}}}\\
+\big(\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)\big(\big\lVert({\bm{u}},{\bm{u}}_{\star})\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert(w,w_{\star})\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}\big)+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}_{\star}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big\lVert w\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}\Big).

Proof.

In view of Lemma
4.1
, we shall first estimate (RHS), defined in (
4.5
). We decompose

{\rm(RHS)}=R_{1}+R_{2}

where
R_{1}
is constituted by terms involving maximum one derivative on
h,\eta,{\bm{u}},{\bm{u}}_{\star},w,w_{\star}
, while

R_{2}:=-(\underline{h}+h)\left(\big({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}+{\bm{u}}_{\star}\big)\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}})\right)-(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta)\cdot\left(\big({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}+{\bm{u}}_{\star}\big)\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}\right)\\
+\big({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}+{\bm{u}}_{\star}\big)\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\partial_{\varrho}w-\left(({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}+{\bm{u}}_{\star})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta)\right)\cdot({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}^{\prime}+\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}).

Appealing to the incompressibility condition

-(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}-(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta)\cdot({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}^{\prime}+\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}})+\partial_{\varrho}w=0,

we have simply

R_{2}=\left(\big({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}+{\bm{u}}_{\star}\big)\cdot(\nabla_{\bm{x}}h)\right)(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}).

As a matter of fact, this term compensates with
the second addend of
R_{1}
, so that contributions from
h
are not differentiated. By inspecting the remaining terms and
using Lemma
A.3
, we infer that for any
r\geq s_{0}+1/2
and
1\leq j\leq r\leq s-1
,

(4.20)

\big\lVert{\rm(RHS)}\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}\lesssim(1+\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{j,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}})\\
\times\left(\big(\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{j,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{r+1,j+1}}\big)\big(\big\lVert({\bm{u}},{\bm{u}}_{\star})\big\rVert_{H^{r+1,j+1}}+\big\lVert(w,w_{\star})\big\rVert_{H^{r+1,j}}\big)+\big\lVert w\big\rVert_{H^{r+1,j}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}_{\star}\big\rVert_{H^{r+1,j+1}}\right).

Owing to the fact that contributions from
(h,\eta,w,w^{\star})
to
{\rm(RHS)}
are affine, and
\mu\in(0,1]
, we have
for any
r\geq s_{0}+3/2
and
2\leq j\leq r\leq s

(4.21)

\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert{\rm(RHS)}\big\rVert_{H^{r-1,j-1}}\lesssim(1+\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{j-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{r-1,j-1}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{r-1,j-1}})\\
\times\left(\big(\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{j-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}\big)\big(\big\lVert({\bm{u}},{\bm{u}}_{\star})\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert(w,w_{\star})\big\rVert_{H^{r,j-1}}\big)+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert w\big\rVert_{H^{r,j-1}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}_{\star}\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}\right).

For the first estimate, we write (
4.6
) as (
4.7
) with
Q_{0}=0
,
Q_{1}=\sqrt{\mu}\Lambda^{-1}{\rm(RHS)}
and

{\bm{R}}=\begin{pmatrix}-\sqrt{\mu}\underline{h}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\\
\frac{h}{\underline{h}+h}(1+\mu|\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta|^{2})-\mu|\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta|^{2}\end{pmatrix}

where we used the identities

\frac{1+\mu|\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}\eta|^{2}}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}{\partial}_{\varrho}\underline{P}_{\rm eq}=\frac{\underline{h}(1+\mu|\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}\eta|^{2})}{\underline{h}+h}=1+\mu|\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta|^{2}-\frac{h}{\underline{h}+h}(1+\mu|\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta|^{2}).

Product (Lemma
A.3
) and composition (Lemma
A.6
) estimates yield if
r\geq s_{0}+1/2
and
1\leq j\leq r\leq s-1

(4.22)

\big\lVert{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}\leq C(h_{\star},\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{j,\infty}_{\varrho}},\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}},\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}})\times\big(\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}\big);

and, if
r\geq s_{0}+3/2
and
2\leq j\leq r\leq s
, using the tame estimates, we obtain

(4.23)

\big\lVert{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}\leq C(h_{\star},\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{j,\infty}_{\varrho}},\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{r-1,j-1}},\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{r-1,j-1}})\times\big(\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}\big).

Plugging in (
4.8
) the estimates (
4.21
) and (
4.23
) with
(r,j)=(s,k)
, and (
4.22
) with
(r,j)=(s-1,k-1)
, yields (
4.17
).

For the next set of estimates, we notice that, by (
4.6
),

P-P_{{\rm h}}

satisfies (
4.7
) with
{\bm{e}}_{d+1}\cdot{\bm{R}}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{1}}=0
and
Q_{0}+\sqrt{\mu}\Lambda Q_{1}+\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}\cdot{\bm{R}}=\mu{\rm(RHS)}+\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{R}}
where

\widetilde{\bm{R}}:=-\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{\mu}\varrho^{-1}(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\psi\\
{\mu}\varrho^{-1}(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta)\cdot(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\psi)\end{pmatrix},\quad\psi:=\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\eta(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\dd\varrho^{\prime}+\rho_{0}\eta\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}.

Indeed, we have immediately
{\bm{e}}_{d+1}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{R}}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{1}}=0=\partial_{\varrho}\big(P-P_{\rm h})\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{1}}
and we infer

-\nabla_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}^{\mu}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{R}}=\nabla_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}^{\mu}\cdot\big(A^{\mu}\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}(\underline{P}_{\rm eq}+P_{\rm h})\big)

from (integrating by parts as in (
2.5b
))

(4.24)

P_{\rm h}:=\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\varrho^{\prime}h(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}=-\varrho\eta+\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\eta(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\dd\varrho^{\prime}+\rho_{0}\eta\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}.

By product estimates (Lemma
A.3
), we infer immediately for any
r\geq s_{0}+1/2
and
1\leq j\leq r

(4.25)

\big\lVert\widetilde{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}\lesssim\Big(\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{j,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}\Big)\,\Big(\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lvert\eta\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{H^{r+1}_{\bm{x}}}\Big).

Moreover, using
the identity

\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{R}}=\mu\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\cdot{\partial}_{\varrho}(\varrho^{-1}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\psi)+\mu\varrho^{-1}(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\psi

we infer for any
r\geq s_{0}+1/2
and
1\leq j\leq r

(4.26)

\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{R}}\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}\lesssim\mu\Big(\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{j,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}\Big)\\
\,\Big(\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{r+1,j}}+\big\lvert\eta\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{H^{r+2}_{\bm{x}}}\Big).

Finally, recalling
\Lambda^{\mu}=1+\sqrt{\mu}|D|
and introducing

\widetilde{\bm{R}}_{0}:=-\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{\mu}\varrho^{-1}(\underline{h}+(\Lambda^{\mu})^{-1}h)((\Lambda^{\mu})^{-1}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\psi)\\
{\mu}\varrho^{-1}((\Lambda^{\mu})^{-1}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta)\cdot((\Lambda^{\mu})^{-1}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\psi),\end{pmatrix}

proceeding as for (
4.25
) and (
4.26
) and using
\Id-(\Lambda^{\mu})^{-1}=\sqrt{\mu}|D|(\Lambda^{\mu})^{-1}
and
\big\lVert(\Lambda^{\mu})^{-1}\big\rVert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}\to L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}=1
, for any
r\geq s_{0}+1/2
and
1\leq j\leq r
one has

(4.27)

\big\lVert\widetilde{\bm{R}}-\widetilde{\bm{R}}_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}+\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{R}}_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}\lesssim\mu\,\Big(\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{j,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{r,j}}\Big)\\
\times\Big(\big\lVert(\Lambda^{\mu})^{-1}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{r+1,j}}+\big\lvert(\Lambda^{\mu})^{-1}\eta\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{H^{r+2}_{\bm{x}}}\Big).

We obtain (
4.18
), by setting
Q_{0}=\mu{\rm(RHS)}
and
Q_{1}=0
, and plug in (
4.9
) the estimates (
4.21
) with
(r,j)=(s,k)
, and (
4.25
) with
(r,j)=(s-1,k-1)
.
For (
4.19
), we set instead
Q_{0}=\mu{\rm(RHS)}+\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}\cdot\widetilde{\bm{R}}_{0}
and
Q_{1}=0
,
{\bm{R}}=\widetilde{\bm{R}}-\widetilde{\bm{R}}_{0}
, and plug in (
4.9
) the estimates (
4.20
) and (
4.27
) with
(r,j)=(s-1,k-1)
.
∎

4.2.
Small-time well-posedness

We infer small-time existence and uniqueness of regular solutions to the Cauchy problem associated with the non-hydrostatic problem, (
4.1
), proceeding as for the hydrostatic system in Section
3
, that is considering
the system as the combination of a transport-diffusion equation and transport equations, coupled through order-zero source terms (by the estimate (
4.17
) in Corollary
4.2
). Specifically, we rewrite (
4.1
) as

(4.28)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}h+\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}\cdot\big((\underline{h}+h)(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}})\big)

\displaystyle=\kappa\Delta_{{\bm{x}}}h,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}{\bm{u}}+\big((\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}h}{\underline{h}+h})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}+\frac{1}{\varrho}\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}P+\big(1+\frac{{\partial}_{\varrho}P-\varrho h}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}\big)\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}\eta

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}w+\big(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}h}{\underline{h}+h}\big)\cdot\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}w-\frac{1}{\mu}\frac{{\partial}_{\varrho}P-\varrho h}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}

\displaystyle=0,

where
\eta(\cdot,\varrho)=\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}h(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\dd\varrho^{\prime}
and
P
is defined by Corollary
4.2
. Systems (
4.1
) and (
4.28
