) are equivalent (for sufficiently regular data) by the computations of Section
4.1
, and in particular regular solutions to (
4.28
) satisfy
the boundary condition

w\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{1}}=0
and
the incompressibility constraint

(4.29)

(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}\cdot{\bm{u}}+\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}\eta\cdot({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}^{\prime}+\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}})-\partial_{\varrho}w=0

provided these identities hold initially.

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
s_{0}>d/2
,
s,k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
s\geq s_{0}+\frac{5}{2}
and
2\leq k\leq s
. Let
h_{\star},\underline{M},M,\mu,\kappa>0
and
C_{0}>1
. There exists
T>0
such that for any
(\underline{h},\underline{{\bm{u}}})\in W^{k,\infty}((\rho_{0},\rho_{1}))^{1+d}
, and for any initial data
(h_{0},{\bm{u}}_{0},w_{0})\in H^{s+1,k}(\Omega)\times H^{s,k}(\Omega)^{d}\times H^{s,k}(\Omega)
satisfying

•

the following bounds (where
\eta_{0}(\cdot,\varrho):=\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}h_{0}(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\dd\varrho^{\prime}
)

\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{k,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}\leq\underline{M}

M_{0}:=\big\lVert h_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert w_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\leq M,

•

the stable stratification assumption

\inf_{({\bm{x}},\varrho)\in\Omega}\underline{h}(\varrho)+h_{0}({\bm{x}},\varrho)\geq h_{\star},

•

the boundary condition
w_{0}|_{\varrho=\rho_{1}}=0
and the incompressibility condition

-(\underline{h}+h_{0})\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}_{0}-(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta_{0})\cdot({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}^{\prime}+\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}_{0})+\partial_{\varrho}w_{0}=0,

there exists a unique
(h,{\bm{u}},w)\in\mathcal{C}^{0}(0,T;H^{s,k}(\Omega)^{2+d})
and
P\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{s+1,k+1}(\Omega))
solution to (
4.28
)
satisfying the initial data
(h,{\bm{u}},w)\big|_{t=0}=(h_{0},{\bm{u}}_{0},w_{0})
. Moreover, the conditions
w|_{\varrho=\rho_{1}}=P|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}=0
and the incompressibility condition (
4.29
) hold on
[0,T]
(and hence the solution satisfies (
4.1
)) and one has
\eta\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s+1,k}(\Omega))
and
(h,\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta)\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{s+1,k}(\Omega))
and

\mathcal{F}_{s,k,T}:=\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s,k})}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s,k})}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s,k})}+\big\lVert w\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s,k})}\\
+c_{0}\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{s,k})}+c_{0}\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}^{2}\eta\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{s,k})}\leq C_{0}M_{0}

with
c_{0}
a universal constant.

Proof.

Since a very similar proof has been detailed in the hydrostatic framework in Section
3
, we will only briefly sketch the main arguments.
As aforementioned, thanks to Corollary
4.2
, we may consider the contributions of the pressure as zero-order source terms in the energy space displayed in the statement, and (
4.28
) is then interpreted as a standard set of evolution equations.
We now explain how to infer the necessary bounds on all contributions to
\mathcal{F}_{s,k,T}
, assuming enough regularity.

The desired control of
\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s,k})}+c_{0}\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{s,k})}
is a direct consequence of the first equation of (
4.28
), and the regularization properties of the heat semigroup already summoned in Proposition
3.1
. The corresponding control of
\big\lVert\nabla_{x}\eta\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s,k})}+c_{0}\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}^{2}\eta\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{s,k})}
demands an additional structure. We recall (see (
2.3
) or (
4.3
)) that
by the identity (
4.29
) and integrating the first equation of (
4.28
), one has

(4.30)

\partial_{t}\eta+(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta-w=\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}\eta.

By the regularization properties of the heat semigroup, we infer (with
c_{0}
a universal constant)

\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s,k})}+c_{0}\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}^{2}\eta\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{s,k})}\leq\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\frac{1}{c_{0}\kappa^{1/2}}\big\lVert(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta-w\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{s,k})},

and the right-hand side is estimated by product estimates (Lemma
A.3
).
Finally, the desired
a priori
estimates on
\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s,k})}
and
\big\lVert w\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s,k})}
for sufficiently regular solutions follow by the energy method (that is integrating by parts in the variable
{\bm{x}}
) on the second and third equations of (
4.28
), which can be seen as transport equations with source terms. More precisely, by (
4.17
) in Corollary
4.2
, we have the existence and uniqueness of
P\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{s+1,k+1}(\Omega))
, satisfying the bound

\big\lVert P\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{s+1,k+1})}\leq C(h_{\star},\mu,\kappa,\underline{M},\mathcal{F}_{s,k,T})\mathcal{F}_{s,k,T}.

Moreover, the advection velocity is controlled (using Lemma
A.1
,
s-2\geq s_{0}+\frac{1}{2}
,
k\geq 1
) by

\big\lVert\nabla\cdot\big(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}h}{\underline{h}+h}\big)\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\Omega))}\leq C(h_{\star},\kappa,\mathcal{F}_{s,k,T}),

and using commutator (Lemma
A.8
) and composition (Lemma
A.6
) estimates, one has for any
f\in H^{s,k}(\Omega)
, and any
{\bm{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
,
j\in\mathbb{N}
with
0\leq j\leq k
and
|{\bm{\alpha}}|+j\leq s
,

\big\lVert[\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}h}{\underline{h}+h}]\nabla_{\bm{x}}f\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C(h_{\star},\kappa,\underline{M},\mathcal{F}_{s,k,T})\big\lVert f\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}.

It follows

\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert w\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\leq\Big(\big\lVert{\bm{u}}_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert w_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+C\sqrt{T}\Big)\exp(CT),

with
C=C(h_{\star},\mu,\kappa,\underline{M},\mathcal{F}_{s,k,T})
.

Altogether, and using standard continuity arguments, we find that for any
C_{0}>1
we can restrict the time
T=T(h_{\star},\mu,\kappa,\underline{M},C_{0}M_{0})>0
so that all sufficiently regular solutions to (
4.28
) satisfy the bound
\mathcal{F}_{s,k,T}\leq C_{0}M_{0}
.
We may infer the existence of solutions using for instance the parabolic regularization approach (see the closing paragraph of Section
3
), and uniqueness is straightforward. This concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.4
.

Proposition
4.3
does not provide any lower bound on the time of existence (and control) of solutions with respect to either
\mu\ll 1
or
\kappa\ll 1
, hence the “small-time” terminology.

4.3.
Quasi-linearization of the non-hydrostatic system

In this section we extract the leading-order terms of the equations satisfied by the spatial derivatives of the solutions to system (
4.28
). This will allow us to obtain improved energy estimates in the subsequent section. Notice that starting from here, our study is restricted to the situation
k=s
.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
s,k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
k=s>\frac{5}{2}+\frac{d}{2}
and
\underline{M},M,h_{\star}>0
. Then there exists
C>0
such that
for any
\mu,\kappa\in(0,1]
, and
for any
(\underline{h},\underline{{\bm{u}}})\in W^{k,\infty}((\rho_{0},\rho_{1}))\times W^{k+1,\infty}((\rho_{0},\rho_{1}))
satisfying

\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{k,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k,\infty}_{\varrho}}\leq\underline{M}\,;

and any
(h,{\bm{u}},w)\in L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s,k}(\Omega)^{d+2})
solution to (
4.28
) (with
P
defined by Corollary
4.2
)
with any
T>0
and satisfying for almost every
t\in[0,T]
,

\big\lVert h(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}+\big\lVert\eta(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lvert\eta\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}(t,\cdot)\big\rvert_{H^{s}_{\bm{x}}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert w(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert h(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\leq M

(where
\eta(t,{\bm{x}},\varrho):=\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}h(t,{\bm{x}},\varrho^{\prime})\dd\varrho^{\prime}
) and

\inf_{(t,{\bm{x}},\varrho)\in(0,T)\times\Omega}\underline{h}(\varrho)+h(t,{\bm{x}},\varrho)\geq h_{\star},

the following results hold.

Denote, for any multi-index
{\bm{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
and any
j\in\mathbb{N}
such that
|{\bm{\alpha}}|+j\leq s
,
h^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}={\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}h,\,\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}={\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}\eta,\,{\bm{u}}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}={\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\bm{u}},\,w^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}={\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}w
, and
P_{\rm nh}^{({\bm{\alpha}})}={\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}P_{\rm nh}
, with

P_{\rm nh}:=P-P_{\rm h},\qquad P_{\rm h}:=\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\varrho^{\prime}h(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}.

We have

(4.31a)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}-w^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}-\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{R}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j},

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+\Big\langle\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}\dd\varrho^{\prime}\Big\rangle_{j=0}-\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}

\displaystyle=R_{{\bm{\alpha}},j},

\displaystyle\partial_{t}{\bm{u}}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+\big(({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h}{\underline{h}+h})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+\Big\langle\frac{\rho_{0}}{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}+\frac{1}{\varrho}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}\dd\varrho^{\prime}\Big\rangle_{j=0}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\varrho}{\nabla_{\bm{x}}P_{\rm nh}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}}+\frac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}{\partial}_{\varrho}P_{\rm nh}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}

\displaystyle={\bm{R}}^{\rm nh}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j},

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mu}\left(\partial_{t}w^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h}{\underline{h}+h})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}w^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}\right)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\frac{{\partial}_{\varrho}P_{\rm nh}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}

\displaystyle=R^{\rm nh}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j},

\displaystyle-{\partial}_{\varrho}w^{({\bm{\alpha}})}+(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta)\cdot({\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)})

\displaystyle+(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}})h^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+(\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}

\displaystyle=R^{\rm div}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j},

where for almost every
t\in[0,T]
, one has
(R_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}(t,\cdot),{\bm{R}}^{\rm nh}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}(t,\cdot),R^{\rm nh}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}(t,\cdot),R^{\rm div}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}(t,\cdot))\in L^{2}(\Omega)^{d+3}
and
R_{{\bm{\alpha}},0}(t,\cdot)\in\mathcal{C}((\rho_{0},\rho_{1});L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
, and

(4.31b)

\displaystyle\big\lVert R_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lvert R_{{\bm{\alpha}},0}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}+\big\lVert\widetilde{R}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert R^{\rm div}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C\,M\,,

\displaystyle\big\lVert{\bm{R}}^{\rm nh}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert R^{\rm nh}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C\,M\,\big(1+\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)

\displaystyle+C\,\big(\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)\big(M+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}_{\star}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert w_{\star}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}\big)\,,

and

(4.32a)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}h^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}

\displaystyle=\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}h^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+r_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}+\nabla_{{\bm{x}}}\cdot{\bm{r}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j},

where for almost every
t\in[0,T]
, one has
(r_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}(t,\cdot),{\bm{r}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}(t,\cdot))\in L^{2}(\Omega)^{1+d}
and

(4.32b)

\displaystyle\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert r_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert{\bm{r}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\,M.

Proof.

Let us first point out that the estimates for
\big\lvert R_{{\bm{\alpha}},0}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}
,
\big\lVert R_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}
,
\big\lVert r_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}
and
\big\lVert{\bm{r}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}
have been stated and proved in Lemma
3.3
. Thus we only need to focus on the other terms.
In the following, we denote
s_{0}=s-\frac{5}{2}>\frac{d}{2}
.

Using the identity (already pointed out in (
4.30
))

\partial_{t}\eta+(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta-w=\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}\eta,

and the commutator estimate in Lemma
A.8
, we find immediately

\widetilde{R}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}=[{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}},\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta,\qquad\big\lVert\widetilde{R}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\big(\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)\big\lVert\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}.

Using the decomposition
P=P_{\rm h}+P_{\rm nh}
as in Corollary
4.2
and the identity (
4.24
) we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{x}}P_{\rm h}:=\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\varrho^{\prime}\nabla_{\bm{x}}h(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}=-\varrho\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta+\rho_{0}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}+\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\,\dd\varrho^{\prime},

and hence the evolution equation for
{\bm{u}}
reads

{\partial}_{t}{\bm{u}}+\big((\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h}{\underline{h}+h})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}+\frac{\rho_{0}}{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}+\frac{1}{\varrho}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}\\
+\frac{1}{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}{{P_{\rm nh}}}+\frac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}{\partial}_{\varrho}{{P_{\rm nh}}}=0.

Differentiating
{\bm{\alpha}}
times with respect to
{\bm{x}}
and
j
times with respect to
\varrho
yields the corresponding equations in (
4.31a
), with remainder terms

{\bm{R}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}^{\rm nh}:={\bm{R}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}-\big[{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}},\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}\big]{\partial}_{\varrho}P_{\rm nh},

using the notation
{\bm{R}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}
for the hydrostatic contributions introduced in Lemma
3.3
. The first
addends have been estimated in Lemma
3.3
, (
3.5b
) (when
j=0
) and (
3.6b
) (when
j\geq 1
). We now estimate the second addend as follows.
By the commutator estimate in Lemma
A.8
with
k=s\geq s_{0}+3/2
, we have

\big\lVert\big[{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}},\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}\big]{\partial}_{\varrho}P_{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\big\lVert\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big\lVert{\partial}_{\varrho}P_{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}.

Then by tame product estimate Lemma
A.3
and composition estimates in Lemma
A.6
,
we have

\big\lVert\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\leq C(h_{\star},\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{k,\infty}},\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}})(\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}})

and there remains to use estimate (
4.18
) in Corollary
4.2
to infer

\big\lVert{\bm{R}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C(h_{\star},\underline{M},M)\,M\,\big(1+\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)\\
+C(h_{\star},\underline{M},M)\,\sqrt{\mu}\,(\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}})\big(M+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}_{\star}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert w_{\star}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}\big).

Now consider

R^{\rm nh}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}:=-\sqrt{\mu}[{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}},\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}w+\kappa\sqrt{\mu}\left[{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}},\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h}{\underline{h}+h}\right]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}w+\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}[{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}},\tfrac{1}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}]{\partial}_{\varrho}P.

We have, by Lemma
A.8
with
k=s\geq s_{0}+3/2
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert[{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}},\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}w\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\sqrt{\mu}\big(\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}w\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}},

and similarly, using tame product estimate Lemma
A.3
and composition estimates in Lemma
A.6
as above,

\kappa\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert\big[{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}},\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h}{\underline{h}+h}\big]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}w\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq\kappa\sqrt{\mu}\,C(h_{\star},\underline{M},M)(\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}^{2}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}})\big\lVert w\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}.

and

\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\big\lVert[{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}},\tfrac{1}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h)}]{\partial}_{\varrho}P_{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq\,\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}C(h_{\star},\underline{M},M)\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big\lVert{\partial}_{\varrho}P_{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}.

Collecting the above and using estimate (
4.18
) in Corollary
4.2
yields

\displaystyle\big\lVert R_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C(h_{\star},\underline{M},M)\,M\,\big(1+\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad+C(h_{\star},\underline{M},M)\,\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big(M+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}_{\star}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\sqrt{\mu}\big\lVert w_{\star}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}\big).

Finally, we consider the remainder (stemming from differentiating the incompressibility condition (
4.29
))

\displaystyle R^{\rm{div}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}

\displaystyle=\big({\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}(h\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}})-(h^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)})\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}-h\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\big({\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}(({\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}})\cdot(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta))-({\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)})\cdot(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta)-({\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}})\cdot(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)})\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\big({\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}(\underline{h}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}})-\underline{h}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}\big)+\big({\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}(\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta)-\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}\big).

Using Lemma
A.9
for the first two terms and direct estimates for the last to terms, and
k=s\geq s_{0}+5/2
,

\displaystyle\big\lVert R_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}^{\rm{div}}\big\rVert

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}+\big\lVert{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\big\lVert\underline{h}\big\rVert_{W^{k,\infty}_{\varrho}}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}+\big\lVert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rVert_{W^{k,\infty}_{\varrho}}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\big\lVert\underline{h}\big\rVert_{W^{k,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rVert_{W^{k,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert h\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)\big(\big\lVert\eta\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}\big).

This concludes the proof.
∎

4.4.
A priori energy estimates

In this section we provide
a priori
energy estimates associated with the equations featured in Lemma
4.5
. We point out that
such estimates concerning
h^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}
solving the transport-diffusion equation (
4.32a
) have been provided in Lemma
3.4
. Corresponding estimates for
\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta
stemming from the first equation of (
4.31a
) are easily obtained. Hence we consider the remaining equations in (
4.31a
). Specifically, recalling the notation
\dot{\eta}=\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)},\dot{h}=h^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)},\dot{\bm{u}}={\bm{u}}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)},\dot{w}=w^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)},\dot{P}_{\text{nh}}=P_{\text{nh}}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}
,
we consider the following linearized system:

(4.33)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\dot{\eta}+(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}+\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}\big((\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}+(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\dot{\bm{u}}\big)\dd\varrho^{\prime}-\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}

\displaystyle=R,

\displaystyle\varrho\big(\partial_{t}\dot{\bm{u}}+\big(({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h}{\underline{h}+h})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big)\dot{\bm{u}}\big)+\rho_{0}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}+\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\dd\varrho^{\prime}+\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{{P_{\rm nh}}}+\frac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta}{\underline{h}+h}{\partial}_{\varrho}\dot{P_{\rm nh}}

\displaystyle={\bm{R}}^{\rm nh},

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mu}\varrho\big(\partial_{t}\dot{w}+\mu\big({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h}{\underline{h}+h}\big)\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{w}\big)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\mu}}\frac{{\partial}_{\varrho}\dot{P_{\rm nh}}}{\underline{h}+h}

\displaystyle=R^{\rm nh},

\displaystyle-{\partial}_{\varrho}\dot{w}+(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\dot{\bm{u}}+\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\cdot{\partial}_{\varrho}\dot{\bm{u}}-({\partial}_{\varrho}\dot{\eta})\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}+\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\cdot(\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}})

\displaystyle=R^{\rm div},

where we denote as always
\eta(\cdot,\varrho)=\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}h(\cdot,\varrho)\dd\varrho
.

We shall use the following definitions of the spaces
Y^{0}
and
Y^{1}

(4.34)

\displaystyle Y^{0}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{C}^{0}([\rho_{0},\rho_{1}];L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\times L^{2}(\Omega)^{d}\times L^{2}(\Omega)\times L^{2}(\Omega)\ ,\qquad\text{ and }

\displaystyle Y^{1}

\displaystyle:=\left\{(\eta,{\bm{u}},w,P)\in H^{1,1}(\Omega)^{d+3}\ :\ \eta\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\ w\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{1}}=0,\ P\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}=0\right\}.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
M,h_{\star},h^{\star}>0
be fixed. There exists
C(M,h_{\star},h^{\star})>0
such that for any
\kappa>0
and
\mu>0
, and for any
(\underline{h},\underline{{\bm{u}}})\in W^{1,\infty}((\rho_{0},\rho_{1}))^{1+d}
and any
T>0
and

(h,{\bm{u}},w)\in L^{\infty}(0,T;W^{1,\infty}(\Omega))
with
\Delta_{\bm{x}}h\in L^{1}(0,T;L^{\infty}(\Omega))

satisfying (
4.28
) and, for almost any
t\in[0,T]
, the estimate

\big\lVert h(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\bm{x}}L^{2}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\leq M

and the upper and lower bounds

\forall({\bm{x}},\varrho)\in\Omega,\qquad h_{\star}\leq\underline{h}(\varrho)+h(t,{\bm{x}},\varrho)\leq h^{\star};

and
for any
(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}},\dot{w},\dot{P}_{\rm nh})\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];Y^{0})\cap L^{2}(0,T;Y^{1})
and
(R,{\bm{R}}^{\rm nh},R^{\rm nh},R^{\rm div})\in L^{2}(0,T;Y^{0})
satisfying system (
4.33
) in
L^{2}(0,T;Y^{1})^{\prime}
,
the following inequality holds:

\displaystyle\frac{\dd}{\dd t}\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}},\dot{w})+\tfrac{\kappa}{2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle+\rho_{0}\kappa\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C\,(1+\kappa^{-1}\big\lVert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\bm{x}}L^{2}_{\varrho}}^{2})\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}},\dot{w})

\displaystyle+C\,\big(M+\big\lVert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\bm{x}}L^{\infty}_{\varrho}}\big)\big\lVert\dot{P_{\rm nh}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\big(\big\lVert{\partial}_{\varrho}\dot{\eta}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\big)

\displaystyle+\big\lVert\dot{P_{\rm nh}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\big\lVert R^{\rm div}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+C\,\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}},\dot{w})^{1/2}\mathcal{E}(R,{\bm{R}}^{\rm nh},R^{\rm nh})^{1/2},

where we denote

(4.35)

\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}},\dot{w})=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dot{\eta}^{2}+\varrho(\underline{h}+h)|\dot{\bm{u}}|^{2}+\mu\varrho(\underline{h}+h)\dot{w}^{2}\,\dd{\bm{x}}\dd\varrho+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\dot{\eta}^{2}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\,\dd{\bm{x}}.

Proof.

We test the first equation against
\dot{\eta}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{1,1}(\Omega))
and its trace on
\{({\bm{x}},\rho_{0}),\ {\bm{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
against
\rho_{0}\dot{\eta}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}_{\bm{x}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
, the second equation against
(\underline{h}+h)\dot{\bm{u}}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{1,1}(\Omega)^{d})
and the third equation against
\sqrt{\mu}(\underline{h}+h)\dot{w}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{1,1}(\Omega))
. This yields:

\displaystyle\frac{\dd}{\dd t}\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}},\dot{w})+\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\kappa\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\big((\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta},\dot{\eta}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}-\left(\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\,\dd\varrho^{\prime},\dot{\eta}\right)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle{\rm(i)}

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\dot{\bm{u}}\,\dd\varrho^{\prime},\dot{\eta}\right)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big(R,\dot{\eta}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle{\rm(ii)}

\displaystyle\quad-\big(\varrho(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\bm{u}},(\underline{h}+h)\dot{\bm{u}}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\kappa\big(\varrho(\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}})\dot{\bm{u}},\dot{\bm{u}}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle{\rm(iii)}

\displaystyle\quad-\big(\rho_{0}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}},(\underline{h}+h)\dot{\bm{u}}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}-\left(\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\,\dd\varrho^{\prime},(\underline{h}+h)\dot{\bm{u}}\right)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle{\rm(iv)}

\displaystyle\quad-\big(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{P_{\rm nh}},(\underline{h}+h)\dot{\bm{u}}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}-\big(({\partial}_{\varrho}\dot{P_{\rm nh}})\,\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta,\dot{\bm{u}}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big({\bm{R}}^{\rm nh},(\underline{h}+h)\dot{\bm{u}}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle{\rm(v)}

\displaystyle\quad-\mu\big(\varrho(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{w},(\underline{h}+h)\dot{w}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\mu\kappa\big(\varrho(\nabla_{\bm{x}}h\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}})\dot{w},\dot{w}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle{\rm(vi)}

\displaystyle\quad+\big({\partial}_{\varrho}\dot{P_{\rm nh}},\dot{w}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\sqrt{\mu}\big(R^{\rm nh},(\underline{h}+h)\dot{w}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle{\rm(vii)}

\displaystyle\quad-\rho_{0}\left((\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}})(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}),\dot{\eta}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\right)_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}-\rho_{0}\left(\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\dd\varrho^{\prime},\dot{\eta}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\right)_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}

\displaystyle{\rm(viii)}

\displaystyle\quad-\rho_{0}\left(\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\rho_{1}}(h+\underline{h})(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\dot{\bm{u}})\dd\varrho^{\prime},\dot{\eta}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\right)_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}+\big(R|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}},\dot{\eta}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big)_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}

\displaystyle{\rm(ix)}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\big(\varrho({\partial}_{t}h)\dot{\bm{u}},\dot{\bm{u}}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\frac{\mu}{2}\big(\varrho({\partial}_{t}h)\dot{w},\dot{w}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

\displaystyle{\rm(x)}

Some terms have already been treated in the course of the proof of Lemma
3.5
: the second term in (i) and the second term in (viii) require
\kappa>0
; the first terms in (i), (viii) are advection terms; the first addend of (ii) together with the second term in (iv) after integration by parts; the first addend of (iv) with the first term in (ix).
The contributions in (iii) compensate with the first addend of (x), using the first equation of (
4.28
) and, in the same way, the contributions in (vi) compensate with the second addend of (x).
It remains only to deal with the contribution frm the non-hydrostatic pressure terms in (v) and (vii), and remainder terms.

Consider the sum of the first two terms in (v) and the first term in (vii). We integrate by parts in
{\bm{x}}
the first term and in
\varrho
in the last two terms. Thus we have

-\big(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{P_{\rm nh}},(\underline{h}+h)\dot{\bm{u}}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}-\big(({\partial}_{\varrho}\dot{P_{\rm nh}})\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta,\dot{\bm{u}}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big({\partial}_{\varrho}\dot{P_{\rm nh}},\dot{w}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
=\big(\dot{P_{\rm nh}},(\underline{h}+h)\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\dot{\bm{u}}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big(\dot{P_{\rm nh}}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta,{\partial}_{\varrho}\dot{\bm{u}}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}-\big(\dot{P_{\rm nh}},{\partial}_{\varrho}\dot{w}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)},

where we used the identity
h=-{\partial}_{\varrho}\eta
and the boundary conditions
\dot{P_{\rm nh}}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}=\dot{\eta}|_{\varrho=\rho_{1}}=\dot{w}|_{\varrho=\rho_{1}}=0
when integrating by parts with respect to
\varrho
.
Using the last equation in (
4.33
) (stemming from the incompressibility condition), the above term reads

\big(\dot{P_{\rm nh}},(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}})({\partial}_{\varrho}\dot{\eta})-(\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}+R^{\rm div}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

These terms, alike remainder terms

\big|\big(R,\dot{\eta}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\big|+\big|\big(R|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}},\dot{\eta}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big)_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}\big|+\big|\big({\bm{R}}^{\rm nh},(\underline{h}+h)\dot{\bm{u}}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\big|+\sqrt{\mu}\big|(R^{\rm nh},(\underline{h}+h)\dot{w}\big)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\big|\ ,

are bounded by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and using
\rho_{0}h_{\star}\leq\varrho(\underline{h}+h)\leq\rho_{1}h^{\star}
.

Altogether, we obtain the differential inequality

\displaystyle\frac{\dd}{\dd t}\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}},\dot{w})+\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle+\rho_{0}\kappa\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C\,\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}},0)+C\big\lVert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}L^{\infty}_{\bm{x}}}\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}},0)^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle+C\,\big(M+\big\lVert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}\big\rVert_{L^{\infty}_{\varrho}L^{\infty}_{\bm{x}}}\big)\big\lVert\dot{P_{\rm nh}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\big(\big\lVert{\partial}_{\varrho}\dot{\eta}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\dot{\eta}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\big)

\displaystyle+\big\lVert\dot{P_{\rm nh}}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\big\lVert R^{\rm div}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+C\mathcal{E}(\dot{\eta},\dot{\bm{u}},\dot{w})^{1/2}\mathcal{E}(R,{\bm{R}}^{\rm nh},R^{\rm nh})^{1/2}

with
C=C(h_{\star},h^{\star},M)
, and the desired estimate follows straightforwardly.
∎

Remark 4.7
.

Lemma
4.6
will be applied to the system (
4.31a
)-(
4.31b
) appearing in Lemma
4.5
,
when

. A similar result holds for the simplified system when

. The main difference is that the result does not require nor provide the control of the trace
{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}\eta\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}
.

4.5.
Large-time well-posedness

We prove the large-time existence of strong solutions to system (
4.1
). As for the hydrostatic system,
large time
underlines the fact that the existence time that is provided by the following result is uniformly bounded (from below) with respect to the vanishing parameter
\mu\in(0,1]
. Besides, the result below keeps track of the dependency of this large time-scale on the diffusivity parameter
\kappa\in[\mu,1]
.

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
s,k\in\mathbb{N}
be such that

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(t):

Proof.

As for the large-time existence for the hydrostatic system (see Proposition

\mathcal{F}

with

By means of (

By (

5.
Convergence

This section is devoted to the proof of the convergence of regular solutions to the non-hydrostatic equations (
2.1
) towards the corresponding solutions to the limit hydrostatic equations (
2.5
), namely Theorem
2.2
.
Our convergence result holds in the strong sense and “with loss of derivatives”: we prove that the solutions to the approximating (non-hydrostatic) equations converge towards the solutions to the limit (hydrostatic) equations in a suitable strong topology that is strictly weaker than the one measuring the size of the initial data.

For a given set of initial data, we use the apex
{\rm h}
to refer to the solution to the hydrostatic equations (provided by the analysis of Section
3
culminating with Theorem
2.1
), and the apex
{\rm nh}
for the corresponding solution to the non-hydrostatic equations (provided by the analysis of Section
4
, specifically Proposition
4.3
). The apex
{\rm d}
denotes the difference between the non-hydrostatic solution and the hydrostatic one, whose size will be controlled in the limit
\mu\searrow 0
.

While we can appeal to Theorem
2.1
to obtain the existence, uniqueness and control of solutions to the hydrostatic equations over a large time interval, Proposition
4.3
provides only a time interval which
a priori
vanishes as
\mu\searrow 0
, and Proposition
4.8
only applies to sufficiently small initial data. The standard strategy (used for instance in
[
KlainermanMajda82
]
in the context of weakly compressible flows) that we apply here relies on a bootstrap argument to control the difference between the non-hydrostatic solution and the hydrostatic one in the time-interval provided by the hydrostatic solution, from which the existence and control of the non-hydrostatic solution (again, with loss of derivatives) can be inferred. We perform this analysis in Sections
5.1
to
5.3
, where we first provide a consistency result (Lemma
5.1
), then exhibit the (non-hydrostatic) quasilinear structure of the equations satisfied by the difference (Lemma
5.2
), and finally infer the uniform control of the non-hydrostatic solution and the strong convergence towards the corresponding hydrostatic solution (Proposition
5.3
). In a last step, in Section
5.4
, we use this uniform control to offer an improved convergence rate based this time on the structure of the hydrostatic equations (Proposition
5.4
). Propositions
5.3
and
5.4
immediately yield Theorem
2.2
.

5.1.
Consistency

In the following result we prove that solutions to the hydrostatic equations (
2.5
) emerging from smooth initial data satisfy (suitably defining the horizontal velocity and pressure variables) the non-hydrostatic equations (
2.1
), up to small remainder terms.

Lemma 5.1
.

There exists
p\in\mathbb{N}
such that for any
s,k\in\mathbb{N}
with
0\leq k\leq s
, the following holds.
Let
\underline{M},M,h_{\star},h^{\star}>0
be fixed. Then there exists
C_{0}>0
and
C_{1}>0
such that for any
\kappa\in(0,1]
, any
(\underline{h},\underline{{\bm{u}}})\in W^{k+p,\infty}((\rho_{0},\rho_{1}))^{1+d}
satisfying

\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{k+p,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k+p-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}\leq\underline{M},

and any initial data
(h_{0},{\bm{u}}_{0})\in H^{s+p,k+p}(\Omega)
satisfying the following estimate

M_{0}:=\big\lVert\eta_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s+p,k+p}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s+p,k+p}}+\big\lvert\eta_{0}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{H^{s+p}_{\bm{x}}}+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert h_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s+p,k+p}}\leq M

(where we denote
\eta_{0}(\cdot,\varrho):=\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}h_{0}(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\dd\varrho^{\prime}
) and the stable stratification assumption

\inf_{({\bm{x}},\varrho)\in\Omega}h_{\star}\leq\underline{h}(\varrho)+h_{0}({\bm{x}},\varrho)\leq h^{\star},

there exists a unique
(h^{\rm h},{\bm{u}}^{\rm h})\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];H^{s+p,k+p}(\Omega)^{1+d})
strong solution to (
2.5
) with initial data
(h^{\rm h},{\bm{u}}^{\rm h})\big|_{t=0}=(h_{0},{\bm{u}}_{0})
, where

T^{-1}=C_{0}\,\big(1+\kappa^{-1}\big(\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}}^{2}+M_{0}^{2}\big)\big)\,.

Moreover, one has for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\forall({\bm{x}},\varrho)\in\Omega,\qquad h_{\star}/2\leq\underline{h}(\varrho)+h^{\rm h}(t,{\bm{x}},\varrho)\leq 2\,h^{\star},

and, denoting
\eta^{\rm h}(\cdot,\varrho):=\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}h^{\rm h}(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\dd\varrho^{\prime}
and

(5.1)

\displaystyle w^{\rm h}(\cdot,\varrho)

\displaystyle:=-\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{h}(\varrho^{\prime})+h^{\rm h}(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime}))\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})+\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm h}(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\cdot({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}^{\prime}(\varrho^{\prime})+\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime}))\dd\varrho^{\prime}\,,

(5.2)

\displaystyle P^{\rm h}(\cdot,\varrho)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\varrho^{\prime}h^{\rm h}(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\dd\varrho^{\prime}\,,

one has for any
t\in[0,T]
,

(5.3)

\big\lVert\big(h^{\rm h}(t,\cdot),\eta^{\rm h}(t,\cdot),{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}(t,\cdot),w^{\rm h}(t,\cdot),P^{\rm h}(t,\cdot)\big)\big\rVert_{H^{s+1,k+1}}\leq C_{1}\,M_{0}\,,

and

(5.4a)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}h^{\rm h}+\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\big((\underline{h}+h^{\rm h})(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm h})\big)

\displaystyle=\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm h},

\displaystyle\varrho\Big(\partial_{t}{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}+\big((\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm h}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm h}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}^{\rm h}\Big)+\nabla_{\bm{x}}P^{\rm h}+\frac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm h}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm h}}({\partial}_{\varrho}P^{\rm h}+\varrho\underline{h}^{\rm h})

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\mu\varrho\Big(\partial_{t}w^{\rm h}+\big(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm h}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm h}}\big)\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}w^{\rm h}\Big)-\frac{{\partial}_{\varrho}P^{\rm h}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm h}}+\frac{\varrho h^{\rm h}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm h}}

\displaystyle=\mu\,R^{\rm h},

\displaystyle-(\underline{h}+h^{\rm h})\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}-\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm h}\cdot({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}^{\prime}+\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{\rm h})+\partial_{\varrho}w^{\rm h}

\displaystyle=0,

with
R^{\rm h}(t,\cdot)\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];H^{s,k}(\Omega))
and satisfying for any
t\in[0,T]
,

(5.4b)

\big\lVert R^{\rm h}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\leq C_{1}\,M_{0}.

Proof.

From Theorem
2.1
we infer immediately (for
p>2+d/2
) the existence, uniqueness and control of the hydrostatic solution
(h^{\rm h},{\bm{u}}^{\rm h})\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T];H^{s+p,k+p}(\Omega)^{1+d})
, and
C_{0}>0
. From the formula (
5.1
), (
5.2
) and product estimates (Lemma
A.3
) in the space
H^{s+p^{\prime},k+p^{\prime}}(\Omega)
(for
1\leq p^{\prime}\leq p
sufficiently large) we infer the estimate (
5.3
).

We obtain similarly the desired consistency estimate, (
5.4a
)-(
5.4b
), using the identity (recall (
4.24
))

P^{\rm h}+\varrho\eta^{\rm h}=\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\eta^{\rm h}(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\dd\varrho^{\prime}+\rho_{0}\eta^{\rm h}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\,,

and denoting

R^{\rm h}:=\varrho\Big(\partial_{t}w^{\rm h}+\big(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm h}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm h}}\big)\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}w^{\rm h}\Big),

differentiating with respect to time the identity (
5.1
), and using (
2.5
) to infer the control of
{\partial}_{t}{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}
and
{\partial}_{t}w^{\rm h}
.
∎

As a corollary to the above, we can write the equations satisfied by the difference between
(h^{\rm h},{\bm{u}}^{\rm h},w^{\rm h})
, i.e. the maximal solution to the hydrostatic equations emerging from given regular, well-prepared initial data, and
(h^{\rm nh},{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh},w^{\rm nh})
, i.e. the maximal solution to the non-hydrostatic with the same data (see Proposition
4.3
). Specifically, under the assumptions and using the notations of Lemma
5.1
, we have that

h^{\rm d}:=h^{\rm nh}-h^{\rm h};\quad{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}:={\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}-{\bm{u}}^{\rm h};\quad w^{\rm d}:=w^{\rm nh}-w^{\rm h};\quad

satisfies
(h^{d},{\bm{u}}^{\rm d},w^{\rm d})\big|_{t=0}=(0,0,0)
and

(5.5)

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}h^{\rm d}+\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot\big((\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh})h^{\rm d}+(\underline{h}+h^{\rm h}){\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\big)

\displaystyle=\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm d},

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}\eta^{\rm d}+(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}+\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}+\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh})\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm h}+h^{\rm d}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d},

\displaystyle{\partial}_{t}{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}+\big((\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}^{\rm d}+{\frac{\rho_{0}}{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}}+{\frac{1}{\varrho}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+\big(({\bm{u}}^{\rm d}-\kappa(\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh}}-\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm h}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm h}}))\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}^{\rm h}+\frac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}{{P_{\rm nh}}}}{\varrho}+\frac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm nh}}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh})}{\partial}_{\varrho}{{P_{\rm nh}}}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\mu\big(\partial_{t}w^{\rm d}+\big(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh}}\big)\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}w^{\rm d}+({\bm{u}}^{\rm d}-\kappa(\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh}}-\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm h}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm h}}))\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}w^{\rm h}\big)-\frac{{\partial}_{\varrho}P_{\rm nh}}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh})}

\displaystyle=-\mu\,R^{\rm h},

\displaystyle-(\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh})\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}-h^{\rm d}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}-\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}\cdot({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}^{\prime}+\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh})-\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm h}\cdot(\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{\rm d})+\partial_{\varrho}w^{\rm d}

\displaystyle=0,

where we denote as usual
\eta^{\rm h}(\cdot,\varrho)=\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}h^{\rm h}(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\dd\varrho^{\prime}
(and analogously
\eta^{\rm nh}
,
\eta^{\rm d}
), and define the non-hydrostatic pressure
{P_{\rm nh}(\cdot,\varrho):=P^{\rm nh}(\cdot,\varrho)-\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\varrho^{\prime}h^{\rm nh}(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\dd\varrho^{\prime}}
where
P^{\rm nh}
is defined by Corollary
4.2
.

5.2.
Quasi-linearization

In this section we extract the leading order terms of the system (
5.5
), in the spirit of Lemma
4.5
.

Lemma 5.2
.

There exists
p\in\mathbb{N}
such that for any
s,k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
k=s>\frac{5}{2}+\frac{d}{2}
and
\underline{M},M,h_{\star}>0
, there exists
C>0
and
C_{1}>0
such that the following holds.
For any
0<\mu\leq\kappa\leq 1
, and
for any
(\underline{h},\underline{{\bm{u}}})\in W^{k+p,\infty}((\rho_{0},\rho_{1}))^{1+d}
satisfying

\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{k+p,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k+p-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}\leq\underline{M}\,;

and any
(h^{\rm nh},{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh},w^{\rm nh})\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T^{\rm nh}];H^{s,k}(\Omega)^{d+2})
and
P^{\rm nh}\in L^{2}(0,T^{\rm nh};H^{s+1,k+1}(\Omega))
solution to (
4.28
)
with some
T^{\rm nh}>0
and satisfying for any
t\in[0,T^{\rm nh}]

\big\lVert h^{\rm nh}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}+\big\lVert\eta^{\rm nh}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lvert\eta^{\rm nh}(t,\cdot)\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{H^{s}_{\bm{x}}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\mu^{1/2}\big\lVert w^{\rm nh}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\\
+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert h^{\rm nh}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\mu^{1/2}\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm nh}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\leq M

(where
\eta^{\rm nh}(t,{\bm{x}},\varrho):=\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}h^{\rm nh}(t,{\bm{x}},\varrho^{\prime})\dd\varrho^{\prime}
), the stable stratification assumption

\inf_{({\bm{x}},\varrho)\in\Omega}\underline{h}(\varrho)+h^{\rm nh}(t,{\bm{x}},\varrho)\geq h_{\star},

and the initial bound

M_{0}:=\big\lVert\eta^{\rm nh}\big|_{t=0}\big\rVert_{H^{s+p,k+p}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}\big|_{t=0}\big\rVert_{H^{s+p,k+p}}+\big\lvert(\eta^{\rm nh}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}})\big|_{t=0}\big\rvert_{H^{s+p}_{\bm{x}}}+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert h^{\rm nh}\big|_{t=0}\big\rVert_{H^{s+p,k+p}}\leq M,

we have the following.

Denote
(h^{\rm h},{\bm{u}}^{\rm h},w^{\rm h})\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T^{\rm h}];H^{s+1,k+1}(\Omega)^{2+d})
the corresponding strong solution to the hydrostatic equations (
3.1
) (see Lemma
5.1
) satisfying

\big\lVert h^{\rm h}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s+1,k+1}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s+1,k+1}}+\big\lVert\eta^{\rm h}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s+1,k+1}}+\big\lVert w^{\rm h}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s+1,k+1}}\leq C_{1}M_{0}

and, for any multi-index
{\bm{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
and
j\in\mathbb{N}
such that
0\leq|{\bm{\alpha}}|+j\leq s
,

\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}:={\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}\eta^{\rm nh}-{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}\eta^{\rm h};\quad{\bm{u}}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}:={\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}-{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\bm{u}}^{\rm h};\quad w^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}:={\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}w^{\rm nh}-{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}w^{\rm h};\quad

and

{P^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}_{\rm nh}(\cdot,\varrho)={\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}\big(P^{\rm nh}(\cdot,\varrho)-\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\varrho^{\prime}h^{\rm nh}(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\dd\varrho^{\prime}\big)}
.

Then restricting to
t\in[0,\min(T^{\rm h},T^{\rm nh})]
and such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{s,k}:=\big\lVert h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}+\big\lVert\eta^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lvert\eta^{\rm d}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{H^{s}_{\bm{x}}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\mu^{1/2}\big\lVert w^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}

\displaystyle+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\mu^{1/2}\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa^{1/2}M

we have

(5.6a)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+w^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}-\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}})}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{R}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j},

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+\Big\langle\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}(\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh})\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}\dd\varrho^{\prime}\Big\rangle_{j=0}-\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}

\displaystyle=R_{{\bm{\alpha}},j},

\displaystyle\partial_{t}{\bm{u}}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+\big(({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+\Big\langle\frac{\rho_{0}}{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}+\frac{1}{\varrho}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}\dd\varrho^{\prime}\Big\rangle_{j=0}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}P_{\rm nh}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+\frac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm nh}}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh})}{\partial}_{\varrho}P_{\rm nh}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}

\displaystyle={\bm{R}}^{\rm nh}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j},

\displaystyle\mu^{1/2}\left(\partial_{t}w^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}w^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}\right)-\frac{1}{\mu^{1/2}}\frac{{\partial}_{\varrho}P_{\rm nh}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh})}

\displaystyle=R^{\rm nh}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j},

\displaystyle-{\partial}_{\varrho}w^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+(\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh})\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+(\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}

\displaystyle+(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh})h^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm nh})\cdot({\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)})

\displaystyle=R^{\rm div}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j},

where
(R_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}(t,\cdot),{\bm{R}}^{\rm nh}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}(t,\cdot),R_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}^{\rm nh}(t,\cdot),R_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}^{\rm div})\in L^{2}(\Omega)^{d+3}
,
R_{{\bm{\alpha}},0}(t,\cdot)\in\mathcal{C}((\rho_{0},\rho_{1});L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
and

(5.6b)

\displaystyle\big\lVert R_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lvert R_{{\bm{\alpha}},0}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}+\big\lVert\widetilde{R}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert R^{\rm div}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C\,\mathcal{F}_{s,k},

(5.6c)

\displaystyle\big\lVert{\bm{R}}^{\rm nh}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert R^{\rm nh}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C\,\big(\mathcal{F}_{s,k}+\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\mu^{1/2}\kappa\big\lVert\Delta_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)+C\,\mu^{1/2}M,

and

(5.7a)

\partial_{t}h^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}+(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}-\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}h^{({\bm{\alpha}},j)}=r_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}+\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{r}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j},

where
(r_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}(t,\cdot),{\bm{r}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}(t,\cdot))\in L^{2}(\Omega)^{1+d}
and

(5.7b)

\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert r_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert{\bm{r}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C\,\mathcal{F}_{s,k}.

Proof.

Explicit expressions for the remainder terms follow from (
5.5
). Specifically,
the following equation is obtained by combining the second and last equation (recall (
4.30
))

\partial_{t}\eta^{\rm d}+(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm h}-w^{\rm d}=\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}

and hence

\widetilde{R}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}:=-[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}-{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}({\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm h}),

and it follows from product (Lemma
A.3
) and commutator (Lemma
A.8
) estimates

\big\lVert\widetilde{R}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\big(\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert\eta^{\rm h}\big\rVert_{H^{s+1,k}}\big)\,\big(\big\lVert\eta^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big).

Then, from the second equation we have

R_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}:=R^{(i)}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}+R^{(ii)}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}

with
R^{(i)}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}:=-[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}
and

R^{(ii)}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}:=\begin{cases}-\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}[\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}},{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}+[\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}},h^{\rm nh}]\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}+\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}\big({\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm h}+h^{\rm d}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}\big)\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}&\text{if $j=0$,}\\
{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j-1}\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}\big((\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}+(\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh})\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm h}+h^{\rm d}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}\big)&\text{if $j\geq 1$.}\end{cases}

Using Lemma
A.3
, Lemma
A.8
and the continuous embedding
L^{\infty}((\rho_{0},\rho_{1}))\subset L^{2}((\rho_{0},\rho_{1}))\subset L^{1}((\rho_{0},\rho_{1}))
we find

\big\lVert R_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\big(\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert h^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}+\big\lVert\eta^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}+\big\lVert h^{\rm h}\big\rVert_{H^{s+1,k-1}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}\big\rVert_{H^{s+1,k-1}}\big)\\
\times\big(\big\lVert\eta^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}+\big\lVert h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}\big)

where for
j=0
we used the identities (and Lemma
A.1
and Lemma
A.7
,(
3
) and (
3
))

\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}[\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}},{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}+[\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}},h^{\rm nh}]\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\dd\varrho^{\prime}=\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}[\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}};{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh},\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}]+[\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}};h^{\rm nh},\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}]\dd\varrho^{\prime}\\
+\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm d}+(\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}\eta^{\rm nh})(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{\rm d})\dd\varrho^{\prime}+\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}+(\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}\eta^{\rm nh})(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm d})

and

\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}(h^{\rm d}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm h})\dd\varrho^{\prime}=\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}[\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}},\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}]h^{\rm d}+(\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}\eta^{\rm d})(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{\rm h})\dd\varrho^{\prime}+(\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}\eta^{\rm d})(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}).

This yields the desired estimate for
\big\lVert R_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}
and the corresponding estimate for
\big\lvert R_{{\bm{\alpha}},0}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}
relies on the additional estimate (stemming from Lemma
A.7
(
2
) and Lemma
A.1
)

\big\lvert(R^{(i)}_{{\bm{\alpha}},0}+\partial_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d})|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}=\big\lvert[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}};{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}},\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}]\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\bm{x}}}\lesssim\big\lVert{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,1}}\big\lvert\eta^{\rm d}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{H^{s}_{\bm{x}}}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle R^{\rm div}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}

\displaystyle:=[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\underline{h}]\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}+[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}

\displaystyle\quad+[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},h^{\rm nh}]\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}+[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}]h^{\rm d}+\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot({\bm{u}}^{\rm h}-{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}){\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}h^{\rm d}

\displaystyle\quad+[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}]\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}+[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm h}]\cdot{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}+\nabla_{\bm{x}}(\eta^{\rm h}-\eta^{\rm nh})\cdot{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}.

Decomposing
h^{\rm nh}=h^{\rm h}+h^{\rm d}
,
{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}={\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}+{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}
, some manipulations of the terms to exhibit symmetric commutators and the use of Lemma
A.8
and
A.9
lead to

\big\lVert R^{\rm div}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\big(\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{k,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}+\big\lVert{\partial}_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}\\
+\big\lVert h^{\rm h}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm h}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}\big\rVert_{H^{s+1,k+1}}\big)\times\big(\big\lVert{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert\eta^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}+\big\lVert h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}\big)

which concludes the estimate (
5.6b
).

We focus now on
\big\lVert{\bm{R}}^{\rm nh}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}
and
\big\lVert R^{\rm nh}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}
.
We have

\displaystyle{\bm{R}}^{\rm nh}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}

\displaystyle\textstyle:=[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}]{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}+\big\langle{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}\big(\frac{\rho_{0}}{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}+\frac{1}{\varrho}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}\big)\big\rangle_{j\geq 1}

\displaystyle\quad+{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}\big(\big(({\bm{u}}^{\rm d}-\kappa(\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh}}-\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm h}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm h}}))\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}^{\rm h}\big)+[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\tfrac{1}{\varrho}]\nabla_{\bm{x}}{P_{\rm nh}}+[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm nh}}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh})}]{\partial}_{\varrho}{P_{\rm nh}},

\displaystyle R^{\rm nh}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}

\displaystyle:=\mu^{1/2}[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\big(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}-\kappa\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh}}\big)\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}]w^{\rm d}+\mu^{1/2}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}\big(({\bm{u}}^{\rm d}-\kappa(\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh}}-\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm h}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm h}}))\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}w^{\rm h}\big)

\displaystyle\quad-\tfrac{1}{\mu^{1/2}}[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\tfrac{1}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh})}]{\partial}_{\varrho}P_{\rm nh}-\mu^{1/2}{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}R^{\rm h},

where
R^{\rm h}
is the consistency remainder introduced in Lemma
5.1
, (
5.4a
) and estimated in (
5.4b
), namely

\big\lVert{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}R^{\rm h}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim M_{0}\leq M.

Let us estimate each contribution. In the following, we shall use repeatedly that
\mathcal{F}^{s,k}\leq\kappa^{1/2}M
and hence
\big\lVert h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\leq M
. As a consequence, by Lemma
A.6
and triangular inequality,

\big\lVert\tfrac{h^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\leq C(h_{\star},\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{k,\infty}_{\varrho}}\big\lVert h^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}})\big\lVert h^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\leq C(h_{\star},\underline{M},M)M.

By Lemma
A.8
, we have

\big\lVert[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}]{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\mu^{1/2}\big\lVert[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},(\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}]w^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
\lesssim\big(\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)\big(\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}+\mu^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}w^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}\big)\leq C(\underline{M},M)\mathcal{F}_{s,k}.

By Lemma
A.9
, Lemma
A.3
and Lemma
A.6

\displaystyle\big\lVert[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\big(\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh}}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big)]{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\lVert\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\lVert\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big(1+\big\lVert\tfrac{h^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)\mathcal{F}_{s,k}

\displaystyle\leq C(h_{\star},\underline{M},M)\big(M+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)\mathcal{F}_{s,k}.

In the same way, we have

\mu^{1/2}\big\lVert[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\big(\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh}}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big)]w^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C(h_{\star},\underline{M},M)\big(M+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)\mathcal{F}_{s,k}.

When
j\geq 1
,

\big\lVert{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}\big(\frac{\rho_{0}}{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big)\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}\big(\frac{1}{\varrho}\int_{\rho_{0}}^{\varrho}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}\,\dd\varrho^{\prime}\big)\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
\lesssim\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{H^{s-1}_{\bm{x}}}+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}\leq\mathcal{F}_{s,k}.

By Lemma
A.3
, we have

\big\lVert{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}\big(({\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}){\bm{u}}^{\rm h}\big)\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\mu^{1/2}\big\lVert{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}\big(({\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}})w^{\rm h}\big)\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
\lesssim\big\lVert{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big(\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\mu^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}w^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)\leq C(\underline{M},M)\mathcal{F}_{s,k}.

By repeated use of tame estimates in Lemma
A.3
and Lemma
A.5
, we find

\displaystyle\big\lVert{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}\big((\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh}}-\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm h}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm h}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big){\bm{u}}^{\rm h}\big)\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\lVert\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm d}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm h}}+\tfrac{h^{\rm d}\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}}{(\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh})(\underline{h}+h^{\rm h})}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}

\displaystyle\leq C(h_{\star},\underline{M},M)M(\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+M\big\lVert h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}),

and similarly

\mu^{1/2}\big\lVert{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}\big((\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh}}-\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm h}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm h}})\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\big)w^{\rm h}\big)\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C(h_{\star},\underline{M},M)M(\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+M\big\lVert h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}).

Contributions from the pressure remain.
By direct inspection, and since
|{\bm{\alpha}}|+j-1\leq s-1
,

\big\lVert[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\tfrac{1}{\varrho}]\nabla_{\bm{x}}P_{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}P_{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}.

By Lemma
A.8
and since
s=k>\frac{5}{2}+\frac{d}{2}
, using the above and Lemma
A.1

\displaystyle\big\lVert[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm nh}}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh})}]{\partial}_{\varrho}P_{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\lVert\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm nh}}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh})}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big\lVert{\partial}_{\varrho}P_{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(h_{\star},\underline{M},M)\,\big(M+\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)\big\lVert{\partial}_{\varrho}P_{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\big\lVert[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\tfrac{1}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh})}]{\partial}_{\varrho}P_{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\big\lvert\tfrac{1}{\varrho\underline{h}}\big\rvert_{W^{k,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert\tfrac{h^{\rm nh}}{\varrho(\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh})}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)\big\lVert{\partial}_{\varrho}P_{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(h_{\star},\underline{M},M)\,\big\lVert{\partial}_{\varrho}P_{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}.

Altogether, and using
\mathcal{F}_{s,k}\leq\kappa^{1/2}M
and
\mu\leq\kappa
, we find

(5.8)

\big\lVert{\bm{R}}^{\rm nh}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\big\lVert R^{\rm nh}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C(h_{\star},\underline{M},M)\,\big(\mathcal{F}_{s,k}+\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\mu^{-1/2}\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P_{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}\big).

Now, we use Corollary
4.2
, specifically (
4.19
):

\displaystyle\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P_{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(h_{\star},\underline{M},M)\,\mu\,\Big(\big\lVert(\Lambda^{\mu})^{-1}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lvert(\Lambda^{\mu})^{-1}\eta^{\rm nh}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{H^{s+1}_{\bm{x}}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\big\lVert({\bm{u}}^{\rm nh},{\bm{u}}_{\star}^{\rm nh})\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert(w^{\rm nh},w_{\star}^{\rm nh})\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}_{\star}^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big\lVert w^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}\Big).

where we recall the notations
\Lambda^{\mu}:=1+\sqrt{\mu}|D|
,
{\bm{u}}_{\star}^{\rm nh}:=-\kappa\frac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh}}
and
w_{\star}^{\rm nh}:=\kappa\Delta_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm nh}-\kappa\frac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}\cdot\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh}}
.
Then we use on one hand that

\big\lVert(\Lambda^{\mu})^{-1}h^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}\leq\big\lVert h^{\rm h}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}+\mu^{-1/2}\big\lVert h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}\lesssim M+\mu^{-1/2}\mathcal{F}_{s,k},

and, similarly,

\displaystyle\big\lVert(\Lambda^{\mu})^{-1}\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm h}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\mu^{-1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k}}\lesssim M+\mu^{-1/2}\mathcal{F}_{s,k},

\displaystyle\big\lvert(\Lambda^{\mu})^{-1}\eta^{\rm nh}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{H^{s+1}_{\bm{x}}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\lvert\eta^{\rm h}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{H^{s+1}_{\bm{x}}}+\mu^{-1/2}\big\lvert\eta^{\rm d}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{H^{s}_{\bm{x}}}\lesssim M+\mu^{-1/2}\mathcal{F}_{s,k}.

On the other hand,

\big\lVert w^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}\leq\big\lVert w^{\rm h}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}+\big\lVert w^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}\lesssim C(\underline{M},M)M+\mu^{-1/2}\mathcal{F}_{s,k}

where, for the first contribution, we applied the product estimates to the expression in (
5.1
).
Then, we have

\displaystyle\big\lVert{\bm{u}}_{\star}^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\big\lVert\tfrac{\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}}{\underline{h}+h^{\rm nh}}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\leq\kappa\big(\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert h^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(h_{\star},\underline{M},M)\,\big(M+\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big),

\displaystyle\big\lVert w_{\star}^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\kappa\big\lVert\Delta_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}+C(h_{\star},\underline{M},M)\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(h_{\star},\underline{M},M)\,\big(M+\kappa\big\lVert\Delta_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}+\kappa^{1/2}M\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k-1}}\big).

Altogether, this yields

\mu^{-1/2}\big\lVert\nabla^{\mu}_{{\bm{x}},\varrho}P_{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}\leq C_{0}\,\Big(\mu^{1/2}M+\mathcal{F}_{s,k}+\mu^{1/2}\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\mu^{1/2}\kappa\big\lVert\Delta_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\\
+\big(M+\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)\,\big(\mu^{1/2}M+\mathcal{F}_{s,k}\big)\Big).

Plugging this estimate in (
5.8
), using
\mathcal{F}_{s,k}\leq\kappa^{1/2}M
and
\mu\leq\kappa
, we obtain (
5.6c
).

Finally, we set

{\bm{r}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}:=-[{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j},\underline{{\bm{u}}}+{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}]h^{\rm d}-{\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{{\bm{\alpha}}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}\big((\underline{h}+h^{\rm h}){\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\big),\qquad r_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}:=-({\partial}_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{\alpha}}{\partial}_{\varrho}^{j}h^{\rm d})\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}.

By Lemma
A.3
and Lemma
A.8
and since
s\geq s_{0}+\frac{3}{2}
and
2\leq k=s
, we have

\big\lVert{\bm{r}}_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\big(\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)\big\lVert h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s-1,k-1}}+\big(\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{k,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lVert h^{\rm h}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big)\big\lVert{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}

and (by Lemma
A.1
)

\big\lVert r_{{\bm{\alpha}},j}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\big\lVert h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}\big\lVert{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}.

This yields immediately (
5.7b
). The proof is complete.
∎

5.3.
Strong convergence

In this section, we prove that for
\mu
sufficiently small and starting from regular and well-prepared initial data, the solution to the non-hydrostatic equations exists at least within the existence time of the solution to the hydrostatic equation. We also prove the strong convergence of the non-hydrostatic system to the hydrostatic one as
\mu\searrow 0
.

Proposition 5.3
.

There exists
p\in\mathbb{N}
such that for any
s,k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
k=s>\frac{5}{2}+\frac{d}{2}
and any
\underline{M},M,h_{\star},h^{\star}>0
, there exists
C=C(s,k,\underline{M},M,h_{\star},h^{\star})>0
such that the following holds. For any
0<M_{0}\leq M
,
0<\kappa\leq 1
, and
\mu>0
such that

\mu\leq\kappa/(CM_{0}^{2}),

for any
(\underline{h},\underline{{\bm{u}}})\in W^{k+p,\infty}((\rho_{0},\rho_{1}))^{1+d}
satisfying

\big\lvert\underline{h}\big\rvert_{W^{k+p,\infty}_{\varrho}}+\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{W^{k+p-1,\infty}_{\varrho}}\leq\underline{M}\,;

for any initial data
(h_{0},{\bm{u}}_{0},w_{0})\in H^{s,k}(\Omega)^{2+d}
satisfying the boundary condition
w_{0}|_{\varrho=\rho_{1}}=0
and the incompressibility condition

-(\underline{h}+h_{0})\nabla_{\bm{x}}\cdot{\bm{u}}_{0}-(\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta_{0})\cdot({\underline{{\bm{u}}}}^{\prime}+\partial_{\varrho}{\bm{u}}_{0})+\partial_{\varrho}w_{0}=0,

(denoting
\eta_{0}(\cdot,\varrho)=\int_{\varrho}^{\rho_{1}}h_{0}(\cdot,\varrho^{\prime})\dd\varrho^{\prime}
), the bounds

\big\lVert\eta_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s+p,k+p}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s+p,k+p}}+\big\lvert\eta_{0}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{H^{s+p}_{\bm{x}}}+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert h_{0}\big\rVert_{H^{s+p,k+p}}=M_{0}\leq M

and the stable stratification assumption

\inf_{({\bm{x}},\varrho)\in\Omega}h_{\star}\leq\underline{h}(\varrho)+h_{0}({\bm{x}},\varrho)\leq h^{\star},

the following holds. Denoting

(T^{\rm h})^{-1}=C^{\rm h}\,\big(1+\kappa^{-1}\big(\big\lvert\underline{{\bm{u}}}^{\prime}\big\rvert_{L^{2}_{\varrho}}^{2}+M_{0}^{2}\big)\big),

as in Lemma
5.1

there exists a unique strong solution
(h^{\rm nh},{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh},w^{\rm nh})\in\mathcal{C}^{0}([0,T^{\rm h}];H^{s,k}(\Omega)^{1+d})
to the non-hydrostatic equations (
2.1
) with initial data
(h^{\rm nh},{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh},w^{\rm nh})\big|_{t=0}=(h_{0},{\bm{u}}_{0},w_{0})
.
Moreover, one has
h^{\rm nh}\in L^{2}(0,T^{\rm h};H^{s+1,k}(\Omega))
,
\eta^{\rm nh}\in L^{2}(0,T^{\rm h};H^{s+2,k}(\Omega))
and, for any
t\in[0,T^{\rm h}]
, the lower and the upper bounds hold

\inf_{({\bm{x}},\varrho)\in\Omega}\underline{h}(\varrho)+h^{\rm nh}(t,{\bm{x}},\varrho)\geq h_{\star}/3,\quad\sup_{({\bm{x}},\varrho)\in\Omega}\underline{h}(\varrho)+h^{\rm nh}(t,{\bm{x}},\varrho)\leq 3h^{\star},

and the estimate below holds true

\displaystyle\big\lVert\eta^{\rm nh}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\mu^{1/2}\big\lVert w^{\rm nh}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lvert\eta^{\rm nh}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}(t,\cdot)\big\rvert_{H^{s}_{\bm{x}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert h^{\rm nh}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\mu^{1/2}\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm nh}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{s,k})}+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm nh}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{s}_{\bm{x}})}

(5.9)

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{s,k})}+\mu^{1/2}\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}^{2}\eta^{\rm nh}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{s,k})}\leq C\,M_{0},

and
(h^{\rm nh},{\bm{u}}^{\rm nh})
converges strongly in
L^{\infty}(0,T;H^{s,k}(\Omega)^{1+d})
towards
(h^{\rm h},{\bm{u}}^{\rm h})
the corresponding solution to the hydrostatic equations (
2.5
), as
\mu\searrow 0
.

Proof.

We closely follow the proof of Proposition
4.8
and exhibit a bootstrap argument on the functional

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(t):

\displaystyle=\big\lVert\eta^{\rm d}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\mu^{1/2}\big\lVert w^{\rm d}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\big\lvert\eta^{\rm d}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}(t,\cdot)\big\rvert_{H^{s}_{\bm{x}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert h^{\rm d}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}+\mu^{1/2}\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s,k}}

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{s,k})}+\kappa^{1/2}\big\lvert\nabla_{\bm{x}}\eta^{\rm d}\big|_{\varrho=\rho_{0}}\big\rvert_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{s}_{\bm{x}})}

\displaystyle\quad+\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}h^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{s,k})}+\mu^{1/2}\kappa\big\lVert\nabla_{\bm{x}}^{2}\eta^{\rm d}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{s,k})}

where we denote

h^{\rm d}:=h^{\rm nh}-h^{\rm h};\quad\eta^{\rm d}:=\eta^{\rm nh}-\eta^{\rm h};\quad{\bm{u}}^{\rm d}:={\bm{u}}^{\rm nh}-{\bm{u}}^{\rm h};\quad w^{\rm d}:=w^{\rm nh}-w^{\rm h}

with the usual notation for
\eta^{\rm nh}
,
\eta^{\rm h}
, and
w^{\rm h}
is defined by (
5.1
). Denoting by
T^{\star}
the maximal existence time of the non-hydrostatic solution provided by Proposition
4.3
, we set

(5.10)

T^{\rm nh}=\sup\Big\{0<T<\min(T^{\star},T^{\rm h})\,:\;\forall\,t\in(0,T),\;h_{\star}/3\leq\underline{h}(\varrho)+h^{\rm nh}(t,{\bm{x}},\varrho)\leq 3h^{\star}\quad\\
\text{and}\quad\mathcal{F}(t)\leq\mu^{1/2}M_{0}\exp(C_{0}t),\quad\mathcal{F}(t)\leq\kappa^{1/2}M_{0}\Big\},

with
C_{0}
sufficiently large (to be determined later on). We will show by the standard continuity argument that
T^{\rm nh}=\min(T^{\star},T^{\rm h})
, which in turns yields
T^{\star}>T^{\rm h}
and shows the result. Indeed, the converse inequality
T^{\rm nh}=T^{\star}\leq T^{\rm h}
yields a contradiction by Proposition
4.3
and the desired estimates immediately follow from the control of
\mathcal{F}
, the bound

(5.11)

\big\lVert h^{\rm h}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s+1,k+1}}+\big\lVert{\bm{u}}^{\rm h}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s+1,k+1}}+\big\lVert\eta^{\rm h}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s+1,k+1}}+\big\lVert w^{\rm h}(t,\cdot)\big\rVert_{H^{s+1,k+1}}\leq C^{\rm h}M_{0}

provided by Lemma
5.1
, and triangular inequality (when
C
